  into account the best interests of a child directly affected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may exempt the foreign national from the
  payment of any applicable fees in respect of the examination of their
  circumstances under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial criteria

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may not grant permanent resident status
  to a foreign national referred to in subsection 9(1) if the foreign
  national does not meet the province's selection criteria applicable to
  that foreign national.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 8, s. 5
- 2013, c. 16, s. 10
- [2023, c. 19, s. 4](#1411900-1414298){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411900-1414298.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-25.1-20130619.html "Link to previous version of section 25.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public policy considerations

- **[[25.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, in examining the
  circumstances concerning a foreign national who is inadmissible or who
  does not meet the requirements of this Act, grant that person
  permanent resident status or an exemption from any applicable criteria
  or obligations of this Act if the foreign national complies with any
  conditions imposed by the Minister and the Minister is of the opinion
  that it is justified by public policy considerations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may exempt the foreign national from the
  payment of any applicable fees in respect of the examination of their
  circumstances under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial criteria

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may not grant permanent resident status
  to a foreign national referred to in subsection 9(1) if the foreign
  national does not meet the province's selection criteria applicable to
  that foreign national.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The conditions referred to in subsection (1) may
  include a requirement for the foreign national to obtain an
  undertaking or to obtain a determination of their eligibility from a
  third party that meets any criteria specified by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 8, s. 5
- 2012, c. 17, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-25.2-20100629.html "Link to previous version of section 25.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  regulations may provide for any matter relating to the application of
  sections 18 to 25.2, and may include provisions respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} entering, remaining in and re-entering Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} permanent resident status or temporary resident
    status, including acquisition of that status;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} declarations referred to in subsection 22.1(1);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which all or part of the
    considerations referred to in section 24 may be taken into account;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} conditions that may or must be imposed, varied or
    cancelled, individually or by class, on permanent residents and
    foreign nationals;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} undertakings that may or must be given in respect
    of requests made under subsection 25(1) or undertakings referred to
    in subsection 25.2(4), and penalties for failure to comply with
    undertakings;

  - [(d.2)]{.lawlabel} the determination of eligibility referred to in
    subsection 25.2(4); and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} deposits or guarantees of the performance of
    obligations under this Act that are to be given to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may exempt persons or categories of
  persons from the application of section 18 and prescribe the
  conditions under which the exemption applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 26
- 2010, c. 8, s. 6
- 2012, c. 17, s. 15
- 2013, c. 16, s. 11
- 2017, c. 11, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26-20130619.html "Link to previous version of section 26"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Rights and Obligations of Permanent and Temporary Residents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-274598 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of permanent residents

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  permanent resident of Canada has the right to enter and remain in
  Canada, subject to the provisions of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A permanent resident must comply with any conditions
  imposed under the regulations or under instructions given under
  subsection 14.1(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 27
- 2012, c. 19, s. 704
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-27-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 27"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Residency obligation

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  permanent resident must comply with a residency obligation with
  respect to every five-year period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following provisions govern the residency
  obligation under subsection (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a permanent resident complies with the residency
    obligation with respect to a five-year period if, on each of a total
    of at least 730 days in that five-year period, they are

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} physically present in Canada,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} outside Canada accompanying a Canadian citizen
      who is their spouse or common-law partner or, in the case of a
      child, their parent,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} outside Canada employed on a full-time basis by
      a Canadian business or in the federal public administration or the
      public service of a province,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} outside Canada accompanying a permanent resident
      who is their spouse or common-law partner or, in the case of a
      child, their parent and who is employed on a full-time basis by a
      Canadian business or in the federal public administration or the
      public service of a province, or

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} referred to in regulations providing for other
      means of compliance;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is sufficient for a permanent resident to
    demonstrate at examination

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if they have been a permanent resident for less
      than five years, that they will be able to meet the residency
      obligation in respect of the five-year period immediately after
      they became a permanent resident;

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if they have been a permanent resident for five
      years or more, that they have met the residency obligation in
      respect of the five-year period immediately before the
      examination; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a determination by an officer that humanitarian and
    compassionate considerations relating to a permanent resident,
    taking into account the best interests of a child directly affected
    by the determination, justify the retention of permanent resident
    status overcomes any breach of the residency obligation prior to the
    determination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 28
- 2003, c. 22, s. 172(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-28-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 28"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of temporary residents

- **[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  temporary resident is, subject to the other provisions of this Act,
  authorized to enter and remain in Canada on a temporary basis as a
  visitor or as a holder of a temporary resident permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation --- temporary resident

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A temporary resident must comply with any conditions
  imposed under the regulations and with any requirements under this
  Act, must leave Canada by the end of the period authorized for their
  stay and may re-enter Canada only if their authorization provides for
  re-entry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extended period

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a temporary resident who is authorized
  to enter and remain in Canada for an extended period in order to visit
  a Canadian citizen or permanent resident who is their child or
  grandchild, the period referred to in subsection (2) is five years.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 29
- [2023, c. 21, s. 3](#1412066-1414339){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1412066-1414339.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 29"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Work and study in Canada

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national may not work or study in Canada unless authorized to
  do so under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorization

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} An officer may, on application, authorize a foreign
  national to work or study in Canada if the foreign national meets the
  conditions set out in the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Instructions

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1.1), the officer shall refuse
  to authorize the foreign national to work in Canada if, in the
  officer's opinion, public policy considerations that are specified in
  the instructions given by the Minister justify such a refusal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Concurrence of second officer

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} In applying subsection (1.2), any refusal to give
  authorization to work in Canada requires the concurrence of a second
  officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} The instructions referred to in subsection (1.2)
  shall prescribe public policy considerations that aim to protect
  foreign nationals who are at risk of being subjected to humiliating or
  degrading treatment, including sexual exploitation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of work permit

  [(1.41)]{.lawlabel} An officer may revoke a work permit if, in the
  officer's opinion, public policy considerations that are specified in
  instructions given by the Minister justify the revocation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(1.42)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsection (1.41) does not
  affect any other lawful authority to revoke a work permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation or suspension of opinion

  [(1.43)]{.lawlabel} If, in the view of the Department of Employment
  and Social Development, public policy considerations that are
  specified in instructions given by the Minister of Employment and
  Social Development justify it, that Department may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} revoke an assessment provided by that Department
    with respect to an application for a work permit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} suspend the effects of the assessment; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} refuse to process a request for such an assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(1.44)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsection (1.43) does not
  affect any other lawful authority to revoke an assessment referred to
  in that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(1.5)]{.lawlabel} Instructions given under this section shall be
  published in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(1.6)]{.lawlabel} The instructions take effect on the day on which
  they are published, or on any later day specified in the instructions,
  and apply in respect of all applications for authorization to work in
  Canada and requests to provide an assessment with respect to an
  application for a work permit, including those applications and
  requests that were made before that day and for which a final decision
  has not been made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation

  [(1.7)]{.lawlabel} The instructions cease to have effect on the day on
  which a notice of revocation is published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minor children

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every minor child in Canada, other than a child of a
  temporary resident not authorized to work or study, is authorized to
  study at the pre-school, primary or secondary level.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 30
- 2012, c. 1, s. 206
- 2013, c. 33, s. 161, c. 40, s. 235
- 2014, c. 39, s. 307
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-30-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 30"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Status Document]{.HTitleText3} {#h-274660 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status document

- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  permanent resident and a protected person shall be provided with a
  document indicating their status.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act, unless an officer
  determines otherwise

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person in possession of a status document
    referred to in subsection (1) is presumed to have the status
    indicated; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who is outside Canada and who does not
    present a status document indicating permanent resident status is
    presumed not to have permanent resident status.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Travel document

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A permanent resident outside Canada who is not in
  possession of a status document indicating permanent resident status
  shall, following an examination, be issued a travel document if an
  officer is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they comply with the residency obligation under
    section 28;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an officer has made the determination referred to
    in paragraph 28(2)(c); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they were physically present in Canada at least
    once within the 365 days before the examination and they have made
    an appeal under subsection 63(4) that has not been finally
    determined or the period for making such an appeal has not yet
    expired.

#### [Refugee Travel Document]{.HTitleText3} {#h-274673 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designated foreign national

**[[31.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31.1 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
Article 28 of the Refugee Convention, a designated foreign national
whose claim for refugee protection or application for protection is
accepted is lawfully staying in Canada only if they become a permanent
resident or are issued a temporary resident permit under section 24.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 17, s. 16
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-274678 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** The regulations may
provide for any matter relating to the application of sections 27 to 31,
may define, for the purposes of this Act, the terms used in those
sections, and may include provisions respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} classes of temporary residents, such as students and
  workers;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} selection criteria for each class of foreign national
  and for their family members, and the procedures for evaluating all or
  some of those criteria;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} anything referred to in paragraph (b) for which a
  decision or recommendation may or must be made by a designated person,
  institution or organization;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the conditions that must or may be imposed, varied or
  cancelled, individually or by class, on permanent residents and
  foreign nationals, including conditions respecting work or study;

- [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} the conditions that must or may be imposed,
  individually or by class, on individuals and entities --- including
  employers and educational institutions --- in respect of permanent
  residents and foreign nationals, or that must or may be varied or
  cancelled;

- [(d.2)]{.lawlabel} the power to inspect --- including the power to
  require documents to be provided by individuals and entities,
  including employers and educational institutions, for inspection ---
  for the purpose of verifying compliance with the conditions imposed
  under paragraphs (d) and (d.1);

- [(d.3)]{.lawlabel} the consequences of a failure to comply with the
  conditions referred to in paragraphs (d) and (d.1);

- [(d.4)]{.lawlabel} a system of administrative monetary penalties
  applicable to the contravention by an employer of any conditions
  referred to in paragraph (d.1) and the amounts of those penalties;

- [(d.5)]{.lawlabel} the requirement for an employer to provide a
  prescribed person with prescribed information in relation to a foreign
  national's authorization to work in Canada for the employer;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the residency obligation under section 28, including
  rules for calculating applicable days and periods; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which a document indicating
  status or a travel document may or must be issued, renewed or revoked.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 32
- 2012, c. 19, s. 705
- 2013, c. 16, s. 37
- 2014, c. 20, s. 302, c. 39, s. 309
- 2015, c. 36, s. 172
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-32-20141216.html "Link to previous version of section 32"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Inadmissibility]{.HTitleText2} {#h-274694 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules of interpretation

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** The facts that constitute
inadmissibility under sections 34 to 37 include facts arising from
omissions and, unless otherwise provided, include facts for which there
are reasonable grounds to believe that they have occurred, are occurring
or may occur.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Security

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  permanent resident or a foreign national is inadmissible on security
  grounds for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} engaging in an act of espionage that is against
    Canada or that is contrary to Canada's interests;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} engaging in or instigating the subversion by force
    of any government;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} engaging in an act of subversion against a
    democratic government, institution or process as they are understood
    in Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} engaging in terrorism;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} being a danger to the security of Canada;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} engaging in acts of violence that would or might
    endanger the lives or safety of persons in Canada; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} being a member of an organization that there are
    reasonable grounds to believe engages, has engaged or will engage in
    acts referred to in paragraph (a), (b), (b.1) or (c).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2013, c. 16, s. 13\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 34
- 2013, c. 16, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-34-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 34"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Human or international rights violations

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  permanent resident or a foreign national is inadmissible on grounds of
  violating human or international rights for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} committing an act outside Canada that constitutes
    an offence referred to in sections 4 to 7 of the [Crimes Against
    Humanity and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} being a prescribed senior official in the service
    of a government that, in the opinion of the Minister, engages or has
    engaged in terrorism, systematic or gross human rights violations,
    or genocide, a war crime or a crime against humanity within the
    meaning of subsections 6(3) to (5) of the [Crimes Against Humanity
    and War Crimes Act](/eng/acts/C-45.9); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2023, c. 19, s. 5\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} having engaged in conduct that would, in the
    opinion of the Minister, constitute an offence under section 240.1
    of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2023, c. 19, s. 5\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2023, c. 19, s. 5\]]{.Repealed}

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2023, c. 19, s. 5\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 35
- 2013, c. 16, s. 14
- 2017, c. 21, s. 18
- [2022, c. 18, s. 3](#1383247-1385699){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1383247-1385699.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 19, s. 5](#1411902-1414304){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411902-1414304.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-35-20221215.html "Link to previous version of section 35"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sanctions

- **[[35.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national is inadmissible on grounds of sanctions if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} their entry into or stay in Canada is restricted
    under a decision, resolution or measure of an international
    organization of states or association of states, of which Canada is
    a member, that imposes sanctions on a person, entity or [[foreign
    state]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, within the meaning of section 2 of
    the [Special Economic Measures Act](/eng/acts/S-14.5), against which
    or whom Canada has imposed or has agreed to impose sanctions in
    concert with that organization or association;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are currently the subject of an order or
    regulation made under section 4 of the [Special Economic Measures
    Act](/eng/acts/S-14.5); or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they are currently the subject of an order or
    regulation made under section 4 of the [Justice for Victims of
    Corrupt Foreign Officials Act (Sergei Magnitsky
    Law)](/eng/acts/J-2.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, despite section 33, a foreign
  national whose entry into or stay in Canada is no longer restricted
  under a decision, resolution or measure referred to in paragraph
  (1)(a) or who ceases being the subject of an order or regulation
  referred to in paragraph (1)(b) or (c) is no longer inadmissible under
  that paragraph.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 19, s. 6](#1411906-1414315){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411906-1414315.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Serious criminality

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  permanent resident or a foreign national is inadmissible on grounds of
  serious criminality for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} having been convicted in Canada of an offence under
    an Act of Parliament punishable by a maximum term of imprisonment of
    at least 10 years, or of an offence under an Act of Parliament for
    which a term of imprisonment of more than six months has been
    imposed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} having been convicted of an offence outside Canada
    that, if committed in Canada, would constitute an offence under an
    Act of Parliament punishable by a maximum term of imprisonment of at
    least 10 years; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} committing an act outside Canada that is an offence
    in the place where it was committed and that, if committed in
    Canada, would constitute an offence under an Act of Parliament
    punishable by a maximum term of imprisonment of at least 10 years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Criminality

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is inadmissible on grounds of
  criminality for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} having been convicted in Canada of an offence under
    an Act of Parliament punishable by way of indictment, or of two
    offences under any Act of Parliament not arising out of a single
    occurrence;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} having been convicted outside Canada of an offence
    that, if committed in Canada, would constitute an indictable offence
    under an Act of Parliament, or of two offences not arising out of a
    single occurrence that, if committed in Canada, would constitute
    offences under an Act of Parliament; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} committing an act outside Canada that is an offence
    in the place where it was committed and that, if committed in
    Canada, would constitute an indictable offence under an Act of
    Parliament.

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2023, c. 32, s. 53\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transborder criminality

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national is inadmissible on grounds of
  transborder criminality for committing, on entering Canada, a
  prescribed offence under an Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following provisions govern subsections (1) to
  (2.1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence that may be prosecuted either summarily
    or by way of indictment is deemed to be an indictable offence, even
    if it has been prosecuted summarily;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} inadmissibility under subsections (1) to (2.1) may
    not be based on a conviction in respect of which a record suspension
    has been ordered and has not been revoked or ceased to have effect
    under the [Criminal Records Act](/eng/acts/C-47), or in respect of
    which there has been a final determination of an acquittal;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the matters referred to in paragraphs (1)(b)
    and (c) and (2)(b) and (c) do not constitute inadmissibility in
    respect of a permanent resident or foreign national who, after the
    prescribed period, satisfies the Minister that they have been
    rehabilitated or who is a member of a prescribed class that is
    deemed to have been rehabilitated;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a determination of whether a permanent resident has
    committed an act described in paragraph (1)(c) must be based on a
    balance of probabilities; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} inadmissibility under subsections (1) to (2.1) may
    not be based on an offence

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} designated as a contravention under the
      [Contraventions Act](/eng/acts/C-38.7),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for which the permanent resident or foreign
      national is found guilty under the [Young Offenders
      Act](/eng/acts/Y-1), chapter Y-1 of the Revised Statutes of
      Canada, 1985, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} for which the permanent resident or foreign
      national received a youth sentence under the [Youth Criminal
      Justice Act](/eng/acts/Y-1.5).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 36
- 2008, c. 3, s. 3
- 2010, c. 8, s. 7
- 2012, c. 1, s. 149
- [2023, c. 32, s. 53](#1433195-1433950){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433195-1433950.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-36-20121215.html "Link to previous version of section 36"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Organized criminality

- **[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  permanent resident or a foreign national is inadmissible on grounds of
  organized criminality for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} being a member of an organization that is believed
    on reasonable grounds to be or to have been engaged in activity that
    is part of a pattern of criminal activity planned and organized by a
    number of persons acting in concert in furtherance of the commission
    of an offence punishable under an Act of Parliament by way of
    indictment, or in furtherance of the commission of an offence
    outside Canada that, if committed in Canada, would constitute such
    an offence, or engaging in activity that is part of such a pattern;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} engaging, in the context of transnational crime, in
    activities such as people smuggling, trafficking in persons or
    laundering of money or other proceeds of crime.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(a) does not lead to a determination of
  inadmissibility by reason only of the fact that the permanent resident
  or foreign national entered Canada with the assistance of a person who
  is involved in organized criminal activity.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 37
- 2013, c. 16, s. 15
- 2015, c. 3, s. 109(E)
- [2023, c. 32, s. 54(F)](#1433209-1433931){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433209-1433931.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-37-20150226.html "Link to previous version of section 37"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Health grounds

- **[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national is inadmissible on health grounds if their health
  condition

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is likely to be a danger to public health;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is likely to be a danger to public safety; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} might reasonably be expected to cause excessive
    demand on health or social services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(c) does not apply in the case of a
  foreign national who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has been determined to be a member of the family
    class and to be the spouse, common-law partner or child of a sponsor
    within the meaning of the regulations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has applied for a permanent resident visa as a
    Convention refugee or a person in similar circumstances;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is a protected person; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is, where prescribed by the regulations, the
    spouse, common-law partner, child or other family member of a
    foreign national referred to in any of paragraphs (a) to (c).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Financial reasons

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national is
inadmissible for financial reasons if they are or will be unable or
unwilling to support themself or any other person who is dependent on
them, and have not satisfied an officer that adequate arrangements for
care and support, other than those that involve social assistance, have
been made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Misrepresentation

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  permanent resident or a foreign national is inadmissible for
  misrepresentation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for directly or indirectly misrepresenting or
    withholding material facts relating to a relevant matter that
    induces or could induce an error in the administration of this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for being or having been sponsored by a person who
    is determined to be inadmissible for misrepresentation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} on a final determination to vacate a decision to
    allow their claim for refugee protection or application for
    protection; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} on ceasing to be a citizen under

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 10(1)(a) of the [Citizenship
      Act](/eng/acts/C-29), as it read immediately before the coming
      into force of section 8 of the Strengthening Canadian Citizenship
      Act, in the circumstances set out in subsection 10(2) of the
      [Citizenship Act](/eng/acts/C-29), as it read immediately before
      that coming into force,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} subsection 10(1) of the [Citizenship
      Act](/eng/acts/C-29), in the circumstances set out in section 10.2
      of that Act, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} subsection 10.1(3) of the [Citizenship
      Act](/eng/acts/C-29), in the circumstances set out in section 10.2
      of that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following provisions govern subsection (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the permanent resident or the foreign national
    continues to be inadmissible for misrepresentation for a period of
    five years following, in the case of a determination outside Canada,
    a final determination of inadmissibility under subsection (1) or, in
    the case of a determination in Canada, the date the removal order is
    enforced; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} paragraph (1)(b) does not apply unless the Minister
    is satisfied that the facts of the case justify the inadmissibility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inadmissible

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who is inadmissible under this
  section may not apply for permanent resident status during the period
  referred to in paragraph (2)(a).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 40
- 2012, c. 17, s. 17
- 2013, c. 16, s. 16
- 2014, c. 22, s. 42
- 2017, c. 14, s. 25
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-40-20150528.html "Link to previous version of section 40"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of refugee protection ---
foreign national

- **[[40.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national is inadmissible on a final determination under
  subsection 108(2) that their refugee protection has ceased.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of refugee protection ---
  permanent resident

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A permanent resident is inadmissible on a final
  determination that their refugee protection has ceased for any of the
  reasons described in paragraphs 108(1)(a) to (d).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 17, s. 18
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-compliance with Act

**[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** A person is inadmissible
for failing to comply with this Act

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national, through an act or
  omission which contravenes, directly or indirectly, a provision of
  this Act; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a permanent resident, through failing
  to comply with subsection 27(2) or section 28.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inadmissible family member

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  foreign national, other than a protected person, is inadmissible on
  grounds of an inadmissible family member if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} their accompanying family member or, in prescribed
    circumstances, their non-accompanying family member is inadmissible;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are an accompanying family member of an
    inadmissible person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a foreign national referred to in
  subsection (1) who is a temporary resident or who has made an
  application for temporary resident status or an application to remain
  in Canada as a temporary resident,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the matters referred to in paragraph (1)(a)
    constitute inadmissibility only if the family member is inadmissible
    under section 34, 35, 35.1 or 37; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the matters referred to in paragraph (1)(b)
    constitute inadmissibility only if the foreign national is an
    accompanying family member of a person who is inadmissible under
    section 34, 35, 35.1 or 37.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 42
- 2013, c. 16, s. 17
- [2023, c. 19, s. 7](#1411908-1414318){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411908-1414318.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-42-20141120.html "Link to previous version of section 42"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- application to Minister

- **[[42.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on application by
  a foreign national, declare that the matters referred to in section
  34, paragraph 35(1)(b) and subsection 37(1) do not constitute
  inadmissibility in respect of the foreign national if they satisfy the
  Minister that it is not contrary to the national interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- Minister's own initiative

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on the Minister's own initiative,
  declare that the matters referred to in section 34, paragraph 35(1)(b)
  and subsection 37(1) do not constitute inadmissibility in respect of a
  foreign national if the Minister is satisfied that it is not contrary
  to the national interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Considerations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to make a declaration, the
  Minister may only take into account national security and public
  safety considerations, but, in his or her analysis, is not limited to
  considering the danger that the foreign national presents to the
  public or the security of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 16, s. 18
- [2023, c. 19, s. 8](#1411910-1414322){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411910-1414322.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-42.1-20130619.html "Link to previous version of section 42.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** The regulations may
provide for any matter relating to the application of this Division, may
define, for the purposes of this Act, any of the terms used in this
Division, and may include provisions respecting the circumstances in
which a class of permanent residents or foreign nationals is exempted
from any of the provisions of this Division.

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Loss of Status and Removal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-274820 .Subheading}

#### [Report on Inadmissibility]{.HTitleText3} {#h-274821 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preparation of report

- **[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer who is of the opinion that a permanent resident or a foreign
  national who is in Canada is inadmissible may prepare a report setting
  out the relevant facts, which report shall be transmitted to the
  Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral or removal order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is of the opinion that the report is
  well-founded, the Minister may refer the report to the Immigration
  Division for an admissibility hearing, except in the case of a
  permanent resident who is inadmissible solely on the grounds that they
  have failed to comply with the residency obligation under section 28
  and except, in the circumstances prescribed by the regulations, in the
  case of a foreign national. In those cases, the Minister may make a
  removal order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An officer or the Immigration Division may impose any
  conditions, including the payment of a deposit or the posting of a
  guarantee for compliance with the conditions, that the officer or the
  Division considers necessary on a permanent resident or a foreign
  national who is the subject of a report, an admissibility hearing or,
  being in Canada, a removal order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- inadmissibility on
  grounds of security

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a report on inadmissibility on grounds of security
  is referred to the Immigration Division and the permanent resident or
  the foreign national who is the subject of the report is not detained,
  an officer shall also impose the prescribed conditions on the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of conditions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The prescribed conditions imposed under
  subsection (4) cease to apply only when

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is detained;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the report on inadmissibility on grounds of
    security is withdrawn;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a final determination is made not to make a removal
    order against the person for inadmissibility on grounds of security;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Minister makes a declaration under subsection
    42.1(1) or (2) in relation to the person; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a removal order is enforced against the person in
    accordance with the regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 44
- 2013, c. 16, s. 19
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-44-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 44"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Admissibility Hearing by the Immigration Division]{.HTitleText3} {#h-274840 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

**[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** The Immigration Division,
at the conclusion of an admissibility hearing, shall make one of the
following decisions:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} recognize the right to enter Canada of a Canadian
  citizen within the meaning of the [Citizenship Act](/eng/acts/C-29), a
  person registered as an Indian under the [Indian Act](/eng/acts/I-5)
  or a permanent resident;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} grant permanent resident status or temporary resident
  status to a foreign national if it is satisfied that the foreign
  national meets the requirements of this Act;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorize a permanent resident or a foreign national,
  with or without conditions, to enter Canada for further examination;
  or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} make the applicable removal order against a foreign
  national who has not been authorized to enter Canada, if it is not
  satisfied that the foreign national is not inadmissible, or against a
  foreign national who has been authorized to enter Canada or a
  permanent resident, if it is satisfied that the foreign national or
  the permanent resident is inadmissible.

#### [Loss of Status]{.HTitleText3} {#h-274847 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permanent resident

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person loses permanent resident status

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} when they become a Canadian citizen;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on a final determination of a decision made outside
    of Canada that they have failed to comply with the residency
    obligation under section 28;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} when a removal order made against them comes into
    force;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} on a final determination under subsection 108(2)
    that their refugee protection has ceased for any of the reasons
    described in paragraphs 108(1)(a) to (d);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} on a final determination under section 109 to
    vacate a decision to allow their claim for refugee protection or a
    final determination to vacate a decision to allow their application
    for protection; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} on approval by an officer of their application to
    renounce their permanent resident status.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of renunciation

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} A person who loses their permanent resident status
  under paragraph (1)(e) becomes a temporary resident for a period of
  six months unless they make their application to renounce their
  permanent resident status at a port of entry or are not physically
  present in Canada on the day on which their application is approved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of ceasing to be citizen

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person becomes a permanent resident if he or she
  ceases to be a citizen under

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 10(1)(a) of the [Citizenship
    Act](/eng/acts/C-29), as it read immediately before the coming into
    force of section 8 of the Strengthening Canadian Citizenship Act,
    other than in the circumstances set out in subsection 10(2) of the
    [Citizenship Act](/eng/acts/C-29), as it read immediately before
    that coming into force;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subsection 10(1) of the [Citizenship
    Act](/eng/acts/C-29), other than in the circumstances set out in
    section 10.2 of that Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subsection 10.1(3) of the [Citizenship
    Act](/eng/acts/C-29), other than in the circumstances set out in
    section 10.2 of that Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 46
- 2012, c. 17, s. 19
- 2013, c. 16, s. 20
- 2014, c. 22, s. 43
- 2017, c. 14, s. 26
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-46-20150528.html "Link to previous version of section 46"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary resident

**[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** A foreign national loses
temporary resident status

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} at the end of the period for which they are
  authorized to remain in Canada;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on a determination by an officer or the Immigration
  Division that they have failed to comply with any other requirement of
  this Act; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} on cancellation of their temporary resident permit.

#### [Enforcement of Removal Orders]{.HTitleText3} {#h-274871 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Enforceable removal order

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  removal order is enforceable if it has come into force and is not
  stayed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a removal order is enforceable, the foreign
  national against whom it was made must leave Canada immediately and
  the order must be enforced as soon as possible.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 48
- 2012, c. 17, s. 20
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-48-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 48"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}In force

- **[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  removal order comes into force on the latest of the following dates:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day the removal order is made, if there is no
    right to appeal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day the appeal period expires, if there is a
    right to appeal and no appeal is made; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the day of the final determination of the appeal,
    if an appeal is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}In force --- claimants

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a removal order made with
  respect to a refugee protection claimant is conditional and comes into
  force on the latest of the following dates:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day the claim is determined to be ineligible
    only under paragraph 101(1)(e);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a case other than that set out in paragraph (a),
    seven days after the claim is determined to be ineligible;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the claim is rejected by the Refugee Protection
    Division, on the expiry of the time limit referred to in subsection
    110(2.1) or, if an appeal is made, 15 days after notification by the
    Refugee Appeal Division that the claim is rejected;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} 15 days after notification that the claim is
    declared withdrawn or abandoned; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} 15 days after proceedings are terminated as a
    result of notice under paragraph 104(1)(c) or (d).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 49
- 2012, c. 17, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-49-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 49"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay

**[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** A removal order is stayed

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if a decision that was made in a judicial proceeding
  --- at which the Minister shall be given the opportunity to make
  submissions --- would be directly contravened by the enforcement of
  the removal order;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a foreign national sentenced to a term
  of imprisonment in Canada, until the sentence is completed;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for the duration of a stay imposed by the Immigration
  Appeal Division or any other court of competent jurisdiction;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} for the duration of a stay under paragraph 114(1)(b);
  and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} for the duration of a stay imposed by the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Void --- permanent residence

**[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** A removal order that has
not been enforced becomes void if the foreign national becomes a
permanent resident.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No return without prescribed
authorization

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  removal order has been enforced, the foreign national shall not return
  to Canada, unless authorized by an officer or in other prescribed
  circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return to Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a removal order for which there is no right of
  appeal has been enforced and is subsequently set aside in a judicial
  review, the foreign national is entitled to return to Canada at the
  expense of the Minister.

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-274908 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** The regulations may
provide for any matter relating to the application of this Division, and
may include provisions respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} conditions that may or must be imposed, varied, or
  cancelled, individually or by class, on permanent residents and
  foreign nationals;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the form and manner in which an application to
  renounce permanent resident status must be made and the conditions
  that must be met before such an application may be approved;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which a removal order shall be
  made or confirmed against a permanent resident or a foreign national;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which status may be restored;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which a removal order may be
  stayed, including a stay imposed by the Minister and a stay that is
  not expressly provided for by this Act;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the effect and enforcement of removal orders,
  including the consideration of factors in the determination of when
  enforcement is possible;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the effect of a record suspension under the [Criminal
  Records Act](/eng/acts/C-47) on the status of permanent residents and
  foreign nationals and removal orders made against them; and

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the financial obligations that may be imposed with
  respect to a removal order.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 53
- 2012, c. 1, s. 150, c. 17, s. 22
- 2013, c. 16, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-53-20121215.html "Link to previous version of section 53"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Detention and Release]{.HTitleText2} {#h-274921 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immigration Division

**[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** The Immigration Division
is the competent Division of the Board with respect to the review of
reasons for detention under this Division.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrest and detention with warrant

- **[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer may issue a warrant for the arrest and detention of a
  permanent resident or a foreign national who the officer has
  reasonable grounds to believe is inadmissible and is a danger to the
  public or is unlikely to appear for examination, for an admissibility
  hearing, for removal from Canada or at a proceeding that could lead to
  the making of a removal order by the Minister under subsection 44(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrest and detention without warrant

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer may, without a warrant, arrest and detain
  a foreign national, other than a protected person,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} who the officer has reasonable grounds to believe
    is inadmissible and is a danger to the public or is unlikely to
    appear for examination, an admissibility hearing, removal from
    Canada, or at a proceeding that could lead to the making of a
    removal order by the Minister under subsection 44(2); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the officer is not satisfied of the identity of
    the foreign national in the course of any procedure under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention on entry

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A permanent resident or a foreign national may, on
  entry into Canada, be detained if an officer

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} considers it necessary to do so in order for the
    examination to be completed; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has reasonable grounds to suspect that the
    permanent resident or the foreign national is inadmissible on
    grounds of security, violating human or international rights,
    sanctions, serious criminality, criminality, transborder criminality
    or organized criminality.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory arrest and detention ---
  designated foreign national

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} If a designation is made under subsection 20.1(1),
  an officer must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} detain, on their entry into Canada, a foreign
    national who, as a result of the designation, is a designated
    foreign national and who is 16 years of age or older on the day of
    the arrival that is the subject of the designation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} arrest and detain without a warrant --- or issue a
    warrant for the arrest and detention of --- a foreign national who,
    after their entry into Canada, becomes a designated foreign national
    as a result of the designation and who was 16 years of age or older
    on the day of the arrival that is the subject of the designation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a permanent resident or a foreign national is
  taken into detention, an officer shall without delay give notice to
  the Immigration Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 55
- 2012, c. 17, s. 23
- [2023, c. 19, s. 9](#1411912-1414324){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411912-1414324.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 19, s. 15.1](#1411940-1433965){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411940-1433965.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 32, s. 55](#1433211-1433966){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433211-1433966.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-55-20230622.html "Link to previous version of section 55"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release --- officer

- **[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer may order the release from detention of a permanent resident
  or a foreign national before the first detention review by the
  Immigration Division if the officer is of the opinion that the reasons
  for the detention no longer exist. The officer may impose any
  conditions, including the payment of a deposit or the posting of a
  guarantee for compliance with the conditions, that the officer
  considers necessary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of detention --- designated
  foreign national

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a designated foreign national
  who is detained under this Division and who was 16 years of age or
  older on the day of the arrival that is the subject of the designation
  in question must be detained until

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a final determination is made to allow their claim
    for refugee protection or application for protection;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are released as a result of the Immigration
    Division ordering their release under section 58; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they are released as a result of the Minister
    ordering their release under section 58.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- inadmissibility on
  grounds of security

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an officer orders the release of a permanent
  resident or foreign national who is the subject of either a report on
  inadmissibility on grounds of security that is referred to the
  Immigration Division or a removal order for inadmissibility on grounds
  of security, the officer must also impose the prescribed conditions on
  the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of conditions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The prescribed conditions imposed under
  subsection (3) cease to apply only when one of the events described in
  paragraphs 44(5)(a) to (e) occurs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 56
- 2012, c. 17, s. 24
- 2013, c. 16, ss. 22, 36
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-56-20120628.html "Link to previous version of section 56"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of detention

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within
  48 hours after a permanent resident or a foreign national is taken
  into detention, or without delay afterward, the Immigration Division
  must review the reasons for the continued detention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At least once during the seven days following the
  review under subsection (1), and at least once during each 30-day
  period following each previous review, the Immigration Division must
  review the reasons for the continued detention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In a review under subsection (1) or (2), an officer
  shall bring the permanent resident or the foreign national before the
  Immigration Division or to a place specified by it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initial review --- designated foreign
national

- **[[57.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections 57(1) and (2),
  in the case of a designated foreign national who was 16 years of age
  or older on the day of the arrival that is the subject of the
  designation in question, the Immigration Division must review the
  reasons for their continued detention within 14 days after the day on
  which that person is taken into detention, or without delay afterward.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further review --- designated foreign
  national

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection 57(2), in the case of the
  designated foreign national referred to in subsection (1), the
  Immigration Division must review again the reasons for their continued
  detention on the expiry of six months following the conclusion of the
  previous review and may not do so before the expiry of that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In a review under subsection (1) or (2), the officer
  must bring the designated foreign national before the Immigration
  Division or to a place specified by it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 17, s. 25
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release --- Immigration Division

- **[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Immigration Division shall order the release of a permanent resident
  or a foreign national unless it is satisfied, taking into account
  prescribed factors, that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are a danger to the public;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are unlikely to appear for examination, an
    admissibility hearing, removal from Canada, or at a proceeding that
    could lead to the making of a removal order by the Minister under
    subsection 44(2);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Minister is taking necessary steps to inquire
    into a reasonable suspicion that they are inadmissible on grounds of
    security, violating human or international rights, sanctions,
    serious criminality, criminality, transborder criminality or
    organized criminality;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Minister is of the opinion that the identity of
    the foreign national --- other than a designated foreign national
    who was 16 years of age or older on the day of the arrival that is
    the subject of the designation in question --- has not been, but may
    be, established and they have not reasonably cooperated with the
    Minister by providing relevant information for the purpose of
    establishing their identity or the Minister is making reasonable
    efforts to establish their identity; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the Minister is of the opinion that the identity of
    the foreign national who is a designated foreign national and who
    was 16 years of age or older on the day of the arrival that is the
    subject of the designation in question has not been established.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continued detention --- designated
  foreign national

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), on the conclusion of a
  review under subsection 57.1(1), the Immigration Division shall order
  the continued detention of the designated foreign national if it is
  satisfied that any of the grounds described in paragraphs (1)(a)
  to (c) and (e) exist, and it may not consider any other factors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention --- Immigration Division

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Immigration Division may order the detention of a
  permanent resident or a foreign national if it is satisfied that the
  permanent resident or the foreign national is the subject of an
  examination or an admissibility hearing or is subject to a removal
  order and that the permanent resident or the foreign national is a
  danger to the public or is unlikely to appear for examination, an
  admissibility hearing or removal from Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Immigration Division orders the release of a
  permanent resident or a foreign national, it may impose any conditions
  that it considers necessary, including the payment of a deposit or the
  posting of a guarantee for compliance with the conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- designated foreign
  national

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Immigration Division orders the release of a
  designated foreign national who was 16 years of age or older on the
  day of the arrival that is the subject of the designation in question,
  it shall also impose any condition that is prescribed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- inadmissibility on
  grounds of security

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the Immigration Division orders the release of a
  permanent resident or foreign national who is the subject of either a
  report on inadmissibility on grounds of security that is referred to
  the Immigration Division or a removal order for inadmissibility on
  grounds of security, it shall also impose the prescribed conditions on
  the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of conditions

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The prescribed conditions imposed under
  subsection (5) cease to apply only when one of the events described in
  paragraphs 44(5)(a) to (e) occurs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 58
- 2012, c. 17, s. 26
- 2013, c. 16, ss. 23, 36
- [2023, c. 19, s. 10](#1411914-1414326){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411914-1414326.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 19, s. 15.1](#1411942-1433969){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411942-1433969.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 32, s. 56](#1433213-1433970){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433213-1433970.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-58-20230622.html "Link to previous version of section 58"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release --- on request

- **[[58.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on request of a
  designated foreign national who was 16 years of age or older on the
  day of the arrival that is the subject of the designation in question,
  order their release from detention if, in the Minister's opinion,
  exceptional circumstances exist that warrant the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Release --- Minister's own initiative

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on the Minister's own initiative,
  order the release of a designated foreign national who was 16 years of
  age or older on the day of the arrival that is the subject of the
  designation in question if, in the Minister's opinion, the reasons for
  the detention no longer exist.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister orders the release of a designated
  foreign national, the Minister may impose any conditions, including
  the payment of a deposit or the posting of a guarantee for compliance
  with the conditions, that he or she considers necessary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- inadmissibility on
  grounds of security

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister orders the release of a designated
  foreign national who is the subject of either a report on
  inadmissibility on grounds of security that is referred to the
  Immigration Division or a removal order for inadmissibility on grounds
  of security, the Minister must also impose the prescribed conditions
  on the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of conditions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The prescribed conditions imposed under
  subsection (4) cease to apply only when one of the events described in
  paragraphs 44(5)(a) to (e) occurs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 17, s. 27
- 2013, c. 16, s. 36
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-58.1-20120628.html "Link to previous version of section 58.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incarcerated foreign nationals

**[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** If a warrant for arrest
and detention under this Act is issued with respect to a permanent
resident or a foreign national who is detained under another Act of
Parliament in an institution, the person in charge of the institution
shall deliver the inmate to an officer at the end of the inmate's period
of detention in the institution.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minor children

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of this
Division, it is affirmed as a principle that a minor child shall be
detained only as a measure of last resort, taking into account the other
applicable grounds and criteria including the best interests of the
child.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** The regulations may
provide for the application of this Division, and may include provisions
respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} grounds for and criteria with respect to the release
  of persons from detention;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the type of conditions that an officer, the
  Immigration Division or the Minister may impose with respect to the
  release of a person from detention;

- [(a.2)]{.lawlabel} the type of conditions that the Immigration
  Division must impose with respect to the release of a designated
  foreign national who was 16 years of age or older on the day of the
  arrival that is the subject of the designation in question;

- [(a.3)]{.lawlabel} the conditions that an officer, the Immigration
  Division or the Minister must impose with respect to the release of a
  permanent resident or foreign national who is the subject of either a
  report on inadmissibility on grounds of security or a removal order
  for inadmissibility on grounds of security;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} factors to be considered by an officer or the
  Immigration Division; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} special considerations that may apply in relation to
  the detention of minor children.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 61
- 2012, c. 17, s. 28
- 2013, c. 16, s. 36
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-61-20120628.html "Link to previous version of section 61"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Right of Appeal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275022 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Competent jurisdiction

**[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** The Immigration Appeal
Division is the competent Division of the Board with respect to appeals
under this Division.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to appeal --- visa refusal of
family class

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who has filed in the prescribed manner an application to
  sponsor a foreign national as a member of the family class may appeal
  to the Immigration Appeal Division against a decision not to issue the
  foreign national a permanent resident visa.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to appeal --- visa and removal
  order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A foreign national who holds a permanent resident
  visa may appeal to the Immigration Appeal Division against a decision
  to make a removal order against them made under subsection 44(2) or
  made at an admissibility hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to appeal removal order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A permanent resident or a protected person may appeal
  to the Immigration Appeal Division against a decision to make a
  removal order against them made under subsection 44(2) or made at an
  admissibility hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of appeal --- residency
  obligation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A permanent resident may appeal to the Immigration
  Appeal Division against a decision made outside of Canada on the
  residency obligation under section 28.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of appeal --- Minister

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may appeal to the Immigration Appeal
  Division against a decision of the Immigration Division in an
  admissibility hearing.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 63
- 2015, c. 3, s. 110
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-63-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 63"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No appeal for inadmissibility

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  appeal may be made to the Immigration Appeal Division by a foreign
  national or their sponsor or by a permanent resident if the foreign
  national or permanent resident has been found to be inadmissible on
  grounds of security, violating human or international rights,
  sanctions, serious criminality or organized criminality.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Serious criminality

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), serious
  criminality must be with respect to a crime that was punished in
  Canada by a term of imprisonment of at least six months or that is
  described in paragraph 36(1)(b) or (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Misrepresentation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No appeal may be made under subsection 63(1) in
  respect of a decision that was based on a finding of inadmissibility
  on the ground of misrepresentation, unless the foreign national in
  question is the sponsor's spouse, common-law partner or child.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 64
- 2013, c. 16, s. 24
- [2023, c. 19, s. 11](#1411916-1414328){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411916-1414328.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-64-20130619.html "Link to previous version of section 64"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Humanitarian and compassionate
considerations

**[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** In an appeal under
subsection 63(1) or (2) respecting an application based on membership in
the family class, the Immigration Appeal Division may not consider
humanitarian and compassionate considerations unless it has decided that
the foreign national is a member of the family class and that their
sponsor is a sponsor within the meaning of the regulations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition

**[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** After considering the
appeal of a decision, the Immigration Appeal Division shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} allow the appeal in accordance with section 67;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} stay the removal order in accordance with section 68;
  or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} dismiss the appeal in accordance with section 69.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal allowed

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} To
  allow an appeal, the Immigration Appeal Division must be satisfied
  that, at the time that the appeal is disposed of,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the decision appealed is wrong in law or fact or
    mixed law and fact;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a principle of natural justice has not been
    observed; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} other than in the case of an appeal by the
    Minister, taking into account the best interests of a child directly
    affected by the decision, sufficient humanitarian and compassionate
    considerations warrant special relief in light of all the
    circumstances of the case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Immigration Appeal Division allows the appeal,
  it shall set aside the original decision and substitute a
  determination that, in its opinion, should have been made, including
  the making of a removal order, or refer the matter to the appropriate
  decision-maker for reconsideration.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal order stayed

- **[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} To
  stay a removal order, the Immigration Appeal Division must be
  satisfied, taking into account the best interests of a child directly
  affected by the decision, that sufficient humanitarian and
  compassionate considerations warrant special relief in light of all
  the circumstances of the case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Immigration Appeal Division stays the
  removal order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it shall impose any condition that is prescribed
    and may impose any condition that it considers necessary;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all conditions imposed by the Immigration Division
    are cancelled;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it may vary or cancel any non-prescribed condition
    imposed under paragraph (a); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} it may cancel the stay, on application or on its
    own initiative.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reconsideration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Immigration Appeal Division has stayed a
  removal order, it may at any time, on application or on its own
  initiative, reconsider the appeal under this Division.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination and cancellation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Immigration Appeal Division has stayed a
  removal order against a permanent resident or a foreign national who
  was found inadmissible on grounds of serious criminality, criminality
  or transborder criminality, and they are convicted of another offence
  referred to in subsection 36(1), the stay is cancelled by operation of
  law and the appeal is terminated.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 68
- [2023, c. 32, s. 57](#1433215-1433940){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433215-1433940.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-68-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 68"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dismissal

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Immigration Appeal Division shall dismiss an appeal if it does not
  allow the appeal or stay the removal order, if any.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's Appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of an appeal by the Minister respecting a
  permanent resident or a protected person, other than a person referred
  to in subsection 64(1), if the Immigration Appeal Division is
  satisfied that, taking into account the best interests of a child
  directly affected by the decision, sufficient humanitarian and
  compassionate considerations warrant special relief in light of all
  the circumstances of the case, it may make and may stay the applicable
  removal order, or dismiss the appeal, despite being satisfied of a
  matter set out in paragraph 67(1)(a) or (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Immigration Appeal Division dismisses an
  appeal made under subsection 63(4) and the permanent resident is in
  Canada, it shall make a removal order.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision binding

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer, in examining a permanent resident or a foreign national, is
  bound by the decision of the Immigration Appeal Division to allow an
  appeal in respect of the permanent resident or foreign national.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Examination suspended

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister makes an application for leave to
  commence an application for judicial review of a decision of the
  Immigration Appeal Division with respect to a permanent resident or a
  foreign national, an examination of the permanent resident or the
  foreign national under this Act is suspended until the final
  determination of the application.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 70
- 2015, c. 3, s. 111(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-70-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 70"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reopening appeal

**[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** The Immigration Appeal
Division, on application by a foreign national who has not left Canada
under a removal order, may reopen an appeal if it is satisfied that it
failed to observe a principle of natural justice.

### [DIVISION 8]{.HLabel2}[Judicial Review]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275091 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for judicial review

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Judicial review by the Federal Court
  with respect to any matter --- a decision, determination or order
  made, a measure taken or a question raised --- under this Act is,
  subject to section 86.1, commenced by making an application for leave
  to the Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following provisions govern an application under
  subsection (1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application may not be made until any right of
    appeal that may be provided by this Act is exhausted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph 169(f), notice of the
    application shall be served on the other party and the application
    shall be filed in the Registry of the Federal Court ("the Court")
    within 15 days, in the case of a matter arising in Canada, or within
    60 days, in the case of a matter arising outside Canada, after the
    day on which the applicant is notified of or otherwise becomes aware
    of the matter;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a judge of the Court may, for special reasons,
    allow an extended time for filing and serving the application or
    notice;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a judge of the Court shall dispose of the
    application without delay and in a summary way and, unless a judge
    of the Court directs otherwise, without personal appearance; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} no appeal lies from the decision of the Court with
    respect to the application or with respect to an interlocutory
    judgment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 72
- 2002, c. 8, s. 194
- 2015, c. 20, s. 52
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-72-20030702.html "Link to previous version of section 72"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of Minister

**[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may make an
application for leave to commence an application for judicial review
with respect to any decision of the Refugee Appeal Division, whether or
not the Minister took part in the proceedings before the Refugee
Protection Division or Refugee Appeal Division.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judicial review

**[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** Judicial review is
subject to the following provisions:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the judge who grants leave shall fix the day and
  place for the hearing of the application;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the hearing shall be no sooner than 30 days and no
  later than 90 days after leave was granted, unless the parties agree
  to an earlier day;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall dispose of the application without
  delay and in a summary way; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to section 87.01, an appeal to the Federal
  Court of Appeal may be made only if, in rendering judgment, the judge
  certifies that a serious question of general importance is involved
  and states the question.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 74
- 2015, c. 20, s. 53
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 74"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

- **[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the approval of the
  Governor in Council, the rules committee established under section
