  45.1 of the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7) may make rules
  governing the practice and procedure in relation to applications for
  leave to commence an application for judicial review, for judicial
  review and for appeals. The rules are binding despite any rule or
  practice that would otherwise apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inconsistencies

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the event of an inconsistency between this
  Division and any provision of the [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7),
  this Division prevails to the extent of the inconsistency.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 75
- 2002, c. 8, s. 194
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-75-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 75"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 9]{.HLabel2}[Certificates and Protection of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275121 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275122 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** The following definitions
apply in this Division.

[[information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means security or criminal
    intelligence information and information that is obtained in
    confidence from a source in Canada, the government of a foreign
    state, an international organization of states or an institution of
    such a government or international
    organization. ([renseignements]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[judge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[judge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Chief Justice of the Federal
    Court or a judge of that Court designated by the Chief
    Justice. ([juge]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 76
- 2002, c. 8, s. 194
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-76-20030702.html "Link to previous version of section 76"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Certificate]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275129 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral of certificate

- **[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister and the Minister of Citizenship and Immigration shall sign a
  certificate stating that a permanent resident or foreign national is
  inadmissible on grounds of security, violating human or international
  rights, serious criminality or organized criminality, and shall refer
  the certificate to the Federal Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of evidence and summary

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When the certificate is referred, the Minister shall
  file with the Court the information and other evidence that is
  relevant to the ground of inadmissibility stated in the certificate
  and on which the certificate is based, as well as a summary of
  information and other evidence that enables the person named in the
  certificate to be reasonably informed of the case made by the Minister
  but that does not include anything that, in the Minister's opinion,
  would be injurious to international relations, national defence or
  national security or endanger the safety of any person if disclosed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of referral

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Once the certificate is referred, no proceeding under
  this Act respecting the person who is named in the certificate ---
  other than proceedings relating to sections 79.1, 82 to 82.31, 112 and
  115 --- may be commenced or continued until the judge determines
  whether the certificate is reasonable.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 77
- 2002, c. 8, s. 194
- 2005, c. 10, s. 34
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
- 2015, c. 3, s. 112(F), c. 20, s. 54
- [2024, c. 16, s. 101](#1461128-1487719){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1461128-1487719.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-77-20150701.html "Link to previous version of section 77"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- inadmissibility on grounds
of security

- **[[77.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  a certificate stating that a permanent resident or foreign national is
  inadmissible on grounds of security is referred to the Federal Court
  and no warrant for the person's arrest and detention is issued under
  section 81, the Minister of Public Safety and Emergency Preparedness
  shall impose the prescribed conditions on the person who is named in
  the certificate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The prescribed conditions imposed under
  subsection (1) cease to apply only when

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is detained;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the certificate stating that the person is
    inadmissible on grounds of security is withdrawn;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a final determination is made that the certificate
    is not reasonable;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Minister makes a declaration under subsection
    42.1(1) or (2) in relation to the person; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a removal order is enforced against the person in
    accordance with the regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 16, s. 25
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination

**[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** The judge shall determine
whether the certificate is reasonable and shall quash the certificate if
he or she determines that it is not.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 78
- 2005, c. 10, s. 34(E)
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-78-20050404.html "Link to previous version of section 78"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

**[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** An appeal from the
determination may be made to the Federal Court of Appeal only if the
judge certifies that a serious question of general importance is
involved and states the question. However, no appeal may be made from an
interlocutory decision in the proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 79
- 2002, c. 8, s. 194
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-79-20030702.html "Link to previous version of section 79"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal by Minister

- **[[79.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 79, the Minister
  may, without it being necessary for the judge to certify that a
  serious question of general importance is involved, appeal, at any
  stage of the proceeding, any decision made in the proceeding requiring
  the disclosure of information or other evidence if, in the Minister's
  opinion, the disclosure would be injurious to international relations,
  national defence or national security or endanger the safety of any
  person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effects of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The appeal suspends the execution of the decision, as
  well as the proceeding under section 78, until the appeal has been
  finally determined.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 20, s. 55
- [2024, c. 16, s. 102](#1461130-1487721){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1461130-1487721.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-79.1-20150701.html "Link to previous version of section 79.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of certificate

**[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** A certificate that is
determined to be reasonable is conclusive proof that the person named in
it is inadmissible and is a removal order that is in force without it
being necessary to hold or continue an examination or admissibility
hearing.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 80
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-80-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 80"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Detention and Release]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275170 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministers' warrant

**[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** The Minister and the
Minister of Citizenship and Immigration may issue a warrant for the
arrest and detention of a person who is named in a certificate if they
have reasonable grounds to believe that the person is a danger to
national security or to the safety of any person or is unlikely to
appear at a proceeding or for removal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 81
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-81-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 81"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initial review of detention

- **[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  judge shall commence a review of the reasons for the person's
  continued detention within 48 hours after the detention begins.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further reviews of detention --- before
  determining reasonableness

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Until it is determined whether a certificate is
  reasonable, a judge shall commence another review of the reasons for
  the person's continued detention at least once in the six-month period
  following the conclusion of each preceding review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further reviews of detention --- after
  determining reasonableness

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who continues to be detained after a
  certificate is determined to be reasonable may apply to the Federal
  Court for another review of the reasons for their continued detention
  if a period of six months has expired since the conclusion of the
  preceding review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reviews of conditions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person who is released from detention under
  conditions may apply to the Federal Court for another review of the
  reasons for continuing the conditions if a period of six months has
  expired since the conclusion of the preceding review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On review, the judge

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall order the person's detention to be continued
    if the judge is satisfied that the person's release under conditions
    would be injurious to national security or endanger the safety of
    any person or that they would be unlikely to appear at a proceeding
    or for removal if they were released under conditions; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, shall order or confirm the
    person's release from detention and set any conditions that the
    judge considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- inadmissibility on
  grounds of security

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the judge orders the release, under paragraph
  (5)(b), of a person who is named in a certificate stating that they
  are inadmissible on grounds of security, the judge shall also impose
  the prescribed conditions on the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No review of conditions

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The prescribed conditions imposed under
  subsection (6) are not subject to review under subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of conditions

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If a person is subject to the prescribed conditions
  imposed under subsection (6), any variation of conditions under
  subsection 82.1(1) or paragraph 82.2(3)(c) is not to result in the
  person being subject to conditions that do not include those
  prescribed conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of conditions

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The prescribed conditions imposed under
  subsection (6) cease to apply only when one of the events described in
  paragraphs 77.1(2)(a) to (e) occurs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 82
- 2005, c. 10, s. 34
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
- 2013, c. 16, s. 26
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-82-20080222.html "Link to previous version of section 82"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of orders

- **[[82.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  judge may vary an order made under subsection 82(5) on application of
  the Minister or of the person who is subject to the order if the judge
  is satisfied that the variation is desirable because of a material
  change in the circumstances that led to the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calculation of period for next review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of calculating the six-month period
  referred to in subsection 82(2), (3) or (4), the conclusion of the
  preceding review is deemed to have taken place on the day on which the
  decision under subsection (1) is made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arrest and detention --- breach of
conditions

- **[[82.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  peace officer may arrest and detain a person released under section 82
  or 82.1 if the officer has reasonable grounds to believe that the
  person has contravened or is about to contravene any condition
  applicable to their release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appearance before judge

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The peace officer shall bring the person before a
  judge within 48 hours after the detention begins.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the judge finds that the person has contravened or
  was about to contravene any condition applicable to their release, the
  judge shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} order the person's detention to be continued if the
    judge is satisfied that the person's release under conditions would
    be injurious to national security or endanger the safety of any
    person or that they would be unlikely to appear at a proceeding or
    for removal if they were released under conditions;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} confirm the release order; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} vary the conditions applicable to their release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Calculation of period for next review

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of calculating the six-month period
  referred to in subsection 82(2), (3) or (4), the conclusion of the
  preceding review is deemed to have taken place on the day on which the
  decision under subsection (3) is made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

**[[82.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82.3 .sectionLabel}** An appeal from a
decision made under any of sections 82 to 82.2 may be made to the
Federal Court of Appeal only if the judge certifies that a serious
question of general importance is involved and states the question.
However, no appeal may be made from an interlocutory decision in the
proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal by Minister

- **[[82.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 82.3, the Minister
  may, without it being necessary for the judge to certify that a
  serious question of general importance is involved, appeal, at any
  stage of the proceeding, any decision made in the proceeding requiring
  the disclosure of information or other evidence if, in the Minister's
  opinion, the disclosure would be injurious to international relations,
  national defence or national security or endanger the safety of any
  person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effects of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The appeal suspends the execution of the decision
  until the appeal has been finally determined.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 20, s. 56
- [2024, c. 16, s. 103](#1461132-1487723){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1461132-1487723.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-82.31-20150701.html "Link to previous version of section 82.31"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's order to release

**[[82.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82.4 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may, at
any time, order that a person who is detained under any of sections 82
to 82.2 be released from detention to permit their departure from
Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

#### [Protection of Information]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275234 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of information

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following provisions apply to proceedings under any of sections 78 and
  82 to 82.2:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall proceed as informally and
    expeditiously as the circumstances and considerations of fairness
    and natural justice permit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall appoint a person from the list
    referred to in subsection 85(1) to act as a special advocate in the
    proceeding after hearing representations from the permanent resident
    or foreign national and the Minister and after giving particular
    consideration and weight to the preferences of the permanent
    resident or foreign national;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at any time during a proceeding, the judge may, on
    the judge's own motion --- and shall, on each request of the
    Minister --- hear information or other evidence in the absence of
    the public and of the permanent resident or foreign national and
    their counsel if, in the judge's opinion, its disclosure could be
    injurious to international relations, national defence or national
    security or endanger the safety of any person;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} on the request of the Minister, the judge may
    exempt the Minister from the obligation to provide the special
    advocate with a copy of information under paragraph 85.4(1)(b) if
    the judge is satisfied that the information does not enable the
    permanent resident or foreign national to be reasonably informed of
    the case made by the Minister;

  - [(c.2)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of deciding whether to grant an
    exemption under paragraph (c.1), the judge may ask the special
    advocate to make submissions and may communicate with the special
    advocate to the extent required to enable the special advocate to
    make the submissions, if the judge is of the opinion that
    considerations of fairness and natural justice require it;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall ensure the confidentiality of
    information and other evidence provided by the Minister if, in the
    judge's opinion, its disclosure would be injurious to international
    relations, national defence or national security or endanger the
    safety of any person;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} throughout the proceeding, the judge shall ensure
    that the permanent resident or foreign national is provided with a
    summary of information and other evidence that enables them to be
    reasonably informed of the case made by the Minister in the
    proceeding but that does not include anything that, in the judge's
    opinion, would be injurious to international relations, national
    defence or national security or endanger the safety of any person if
    disclosed;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall ensure the confidentiality of all
    information or other evidence that is withdrawn by the Minister;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall provide the permanent resident or
    foreign national and the Minister with an opportunity to be heard;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the judge may receive into evidence anything that,
    in the judge's opinion, is reliable and appropriate, even if it is
    inadmissible in a court of law, and may base a decision on that
    evidence;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the judge may base a decision on information or
    other evidence even if a summary of that information or other
    evidence is not provided to the permanent resident or foreign
    national;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall not base a decision on information
    or other evidence provided by the Minister, and shall return it to
    the Minister, if the judge determines that it is not relevant or if
    the Minister withdraws it; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the judge shall not base a decision on information
    that the Minister is exempted from providing to the special
    advocate, shall ensure the confidentiality of that information and
    shall return it to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(h), reliable and
  appropriate evidence does not include information that is believed on
  reasonable grounds to have been obtained as a result of the use of
  torture within the meaning of section 269.1 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), or cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or
  punishment within the meaning of the Convention Against Torture.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of special advocate

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} If the permanent resident or foreign national
  requests that a particular person be appointed under paragraph (1)(b),
  the judge shall appoint that person unless the judge is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the appointment would result in the proceeding
    being unreasonably delayed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the appointment would place the person in a
    conflict of interest; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person has knowledge of information or other
    evidence whose disclosure would be injurious to international
    relations, national defence or national security or endanger the
    safety of any person and, in the circumstances, there is a risk of
    inadvertent disclosure of that information or other evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the judge's power to appoint a
  person to act as a special advocate in a proceeding includes the power
  to terminate the appointment and to appoint another person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 83
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
- 2015, c. 20, s. 57
- [2024, c. 16, s. 104](#1461134-1487728){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1461134-1487728.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-83-20150701.html "Link to previous version of section 83"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of information on appeal

**[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** Section 83 --- other than
the obligation to provide a summary --- and sections 85.1 to 85.5 apply
in respect of an appeal under section 79, 79.1, 82.3 or 82.31 and in
respect of any further appeal, with any necessary modifications.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 84
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
- 2015, c. 20, s. 58
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-84-20080222.html "Link to previous version of section 84"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Special Advocate]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275266 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}List of persons who may act as special
advocates

- **[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister of Justice shall establish a list of persons who may act as
  special advocates and shall publish the list in a manner that the
  Minister of Justice considers appropriate to facilitate public access
  to it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) does
  not apply to the list.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrative support and resources

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Justice shall ensure that special
  advocates are provided with adequate administrative support and
  resources.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 85
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-85-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 85"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special advocate's role

- **[[85.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  special advocate's role is to protect the interests of the permanent
  resident or foreign national in a proceeding under any of sections 78
  and 82 to 82.2 when information or other evidence is heard in the
  absence of the public and of the permanent resident or foreign
  national and their counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibilities

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A special advocate may challenge

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister's claim that the disclosure of
    information or other evidence would be injurious to international
    relations, national defence or national security or endanger the
    safety of any person; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the relevance, reliability and sufficiency of
    information or other evidence that is provided by the Minister and
    is not disclosed to the permanent resident or foreign national and
    their counsel, and the weight to be given to it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the special advocate is not a
  party to the proceeding and the relationship between the special
  advocate and the permanent resident or foreign national is not that of
  solicitor and client.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of communications with
  special advocate

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} However, a communication between the permanent
  resident or foreign national or their counsel and the special advocate
  that would be subject to solicitor-client privilege if the
  relationship were one of solicitor and client is deemed to be subject
  to solicitor-client privilege. For greater certainty, in respect of
  that communication, the special advocate is not a compellable witness
  in any proceeding.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
- [2024, c. 16, s. 105](#1461141-1487730){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1461141-1487730.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-85.1-20080222.html "Link to previous version of section 85.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

**[[85.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85.2 .sectionLabel}** A special advocate
may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} make oral and written submissions with respect to the
  information and other evidence that is provided by the Minister and is
  not disclosed to the permanent resident or foreign national and their
  counsel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} participate in, and cross-examine witnesses who
  testify during, any part of the proceeding that is held in the absence
  of the public and of the permanent resident or foreign national and
  their counsel; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} exercise, with the judge's authorization, any other
  powers that are necessary to protect the interests of the permanent
  resident or foreign national.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

**[[85.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85.3 .sectionLabel}** A special advocate is
not personally liable for anything they do or omit to do in good faith
under this Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to provide information

- **[[85.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to paragraph 83(1)(c.1), the
  Minister shall, within a period set by the judge,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide the special advocate with a copy of the
    information and other evidence that is relevant to the case made by
    the Minister in a proceeding under any of sections 78 and 82 to
    82.2, on which the certificate or warrant is based and that has been
    filed with the Federal Court, but that is not disclosed to the
    permanent resident or foreign national and their counsel; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide the special advocate with a copy of any
    other information that is in the Minister's possession and that is
    relevant to the case made by the Minister in a proceeding under any
    of sections 78 and 82 to 82.2, but on which the certificate or
    warrant is not based and that has not been filed with the Federal
    Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restrictions on communications ---
  special advocate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} After that information or other evidence is received
  by the special advocate, the special advocate may, during the
  remainder of the proceeding, communicate with another person about the
  proceeding only with the judge's authorization and subject to any
  conditions that the judge considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restrictions on communications ---
  other persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the special advocate is authorized to communicate
  with a person, the judge may prohibit that person from communicating
  with anyone else about the proceeding during the remainder of the
  proceeding or may impose conditions with respect to such a
  communication during that period.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
- 2015, c. 20, s. 59
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-85.4-20080222.html "Link to previous version of section 85.4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure and communication prohibited

**[[85.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85.5 .sectionLabel}** With the exception of
communications authorized by a judge, no person shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} disclose information or other evidence that is
  disclosed to them under section 85.4 and that is treated as
  confidential by the judge presiding at the proceeding; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} communicate with another person about the content of
  any part of a proceeding under any of sections 78 and 82 to 82.2 that
  is heard in the absence of the public and of the permanent resident or
  foreign national and their counsel.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

- **[[85.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Justice of the Federal
  Court of Appeal and the Chief Justice of the Federal Court may each
  establish a committee to make rules governing the practice and
  procedure in relation to the participation of special advocates in
  proceedings before the court over which they preside. The rules are
  binding despite any rule of practice that would otherwise apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of committees

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any committee established shall be composed of the
  Chief Justice of the Federal Court of Appeal or the Chief Justice of
  the Federal Court, as the case may be, the Attorney General of Canada
  or one or more representatives of the Attorney General of Canada, and
  one or more members of the bar of any province who have experience in
  a field of law relevant to those types of proceedings. The Chief
  Justices may also designate additional members of their respective
  committees.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Justices shall preside

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Justice of the Federal Court of Appeal and
  the Chief Justice of the Federal Court --- or a member designated by
  them --- shall preside over their respective committees.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

#### [Other Proceedings]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275326 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for non-disclosure

**[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may, during
an admissibility hearing, a detention review or an appeal before the
Immigration Appeal Division, apply for the non-disclosure of information
or other evidence. Sections 83 and 85.1 to 85.5 apply to the proceeding
with any necessary modifications, including that a reference to "judge"
be read as a reference to the applicable Division of the Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 86
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-86-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 86"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judicial review

- **[[86.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, at any stage of
  the proceeding, apply for judicial review of any decision made in a
  proceeding referred to in section 86 requiring the disclosure of
  information or other evidence if, in the Minister's opinion, the
  disclosure would be injurious to international relations, national
  defence or national security or endanger the safety of any person. The
  application may be made without an application for leave.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effects of judicial review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The making of the application suspends the execution
  of the decision and, except in the case of a detention review, the
  proceeding referred to in section 86, until the application has been
  finally determined.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 20, s. 60
- [2024, c. 16, s. 106](#1461143-1487732){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1461143-1487732.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-86.1-20150701.html "Link to previous version of section 86.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for non-disclosure ---
judicial review and appeal

**[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may, during
a judicial review, apply for the non-disclosure of information or other
evidence. Section 83 --- other than the obligations to appoint a special
advocate and to provide a summary --- applies in respect of the
proceeding and in respect of any appeal of a decision made in the
proceeding, with any necessary modifications.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 87
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
- 2015, c. 20, s. 60
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-87-20080222.html "Link to previous version of section 87"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal by Minister

- **[[87.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, without it being
  necessary for the judge to certify that a serious question of general
  importance is involved, appeal, at any stage of the proceeding, to the
  Federal Court of Appeal any decision made in a judicial review
  requiring the disclosure of information or other evidence if, in the
  Minister's opinion, the disclosure would be injurious to international
  relations, national defence or national security or endanger the
  safety of any person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effects of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The appeal suspends the execution of the decision, as
  well as the judicial review, until the appeal has been finally
  determined.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 20, s. 60
- [2024, c. 16, s. 107](#1461145-1487734){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1461145-1487734.html"

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special advocate

**[[87.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.1 .sectionLabel}** If the judge during
the judicial review, or a court on appeal from the judge's decision, is
of the opinion that considerations of fairness and natural justice
require that a special advocate be appointed to protect the interests of
the permanent resident or foreign national, the judge or court shall
appoint a special advocate from the list referred to in subsection
85(1). Sections 85.1 to 85.5 apply to the proceeding with any necessary
modifications.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275353 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[87.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may provide for any
  matter relating to the application of this Division and may include
  provisions respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the conditions that must be imposed under
    subsection 77.1(1) or 82(6); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the conditions and qualifications that persons must
    meet to be included in the list referred to in subsection 85(1) and
    the additional qualifications that are assets that may be taken into
    account for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall require that, to be included in the list,
    persons be members in good standing of the bar of a province, not be
    employed in the federal public administration, and not otherwise be
    associated with the federal public administration in such a way as
    to impair their ability to protect the interests of the permanent
    resident or foreign national; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may include provisions respecting those
    requirements.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 3, s. 4
- 2013, c. 16, s. 27
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-87.2-20080222.html "Link to previous version of section 87.2"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 10]{.HLabel2}[General Provisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275365 .Subheading}

#### [Instructions on Processing Applications and Requests]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275366 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[87.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This section applies to applications
  for visas or other documents made under subsections 11(1) and (1.01),
  other than those made by persons referred to in subsection 99(2), to
  sponsorship applications made under subsection 13(1), to applications
  for permanent resident status under subsection 21(1) or temporary
  resident status under subsection 22(1) made by foreign nationals in
  Canada, to applications for work or study permits and to requests
  under subsection 25(1) made by foreign nationals outside Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, this section applies to a
  sponsorship application made under subsection 13(1) to sponsor a
  person referred to in subsection 99(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attainment of immigration goals

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The processing of applications and requests is to be
  conducted in a manner that, in the opinion of the Minister, will best
  support the attainment of the immigration goals established by the
  Government of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Instructions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), the Minister may
  give instructions with respect to the processing of applications and
  requests, including instructions

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establishing categories of applications or requests
    to which the instructions apply;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} establishing conditions, by category or
    otherwise, that must be met before or during the processing of an
    application or request;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establishing an order, by category or otherwise,
    for the processing of applications or requests;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} setting the number of applications or requests, by
    category or otherwise, to be processed in any year; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} providing for the disposition of applications and
    requests, including those made subsequent to the first application
    or request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} An instruction may, if it so provides, apply in
  respect of pending applications or requests that are made before the
  day on which the instruction takes effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, an instruction given under
  paragraph (3)(c) may provide that the number of applications or
  requests, by category or otherwise, to be processed in any year be set
  at zero.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with instructions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Officers and persons authorized to exercise the
  powers of the Minister under section 25 shall comply with any
  instructions before processing an application or request or when
  processing one. If an application or request is not processed, it may
  be retained, returned or otherwise disposed of in accordance with the
  instructions of the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The fact that an application or request is retained,
  returned or otherwise disposed of does not constitute a decision not
  to issue the visa or other document, or grant the status or exemption,
  in relation to which the application or request is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Instructions shall be published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section in any way limits the power
  of the Minister to otherwise determine the most efficient manner in
  which to administer this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2008, c. 28, s. 118
- 2012, c. 17, s. 29, c. 19, ss. 706, 710, c. 31, ss. 311, 314
- [2023, c. 26, s. 286](#1410045-1414342){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410045-1414342.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-87.3-20121215.html "Link to previous version of section 87.3"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Applications for Temporary Resident Visas, Work Permits and Study Permits]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1174017 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order in council

- **[[87.31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.31
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the Governor in Council is of the
  opinion that the government of a foreign state or the competent
  authority of any other territory is unreasonably refusing to issue or
  unreasonably delaying the issuance of travel documents to citizens or
  nationals of that country or territory who are in Canada, the Governor
  in Council may make an order specifying one or more of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} applications for temporary resident visas, work
    permits or study permits, or any combination of those types of
    applications, made by any citizen or national of that foreign state
    or territory are not to be accepted for processing if they are
    received during the period or periods set out in the order or, if a
    period is not set out, during the period that the order is in force;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the processing of any of those types of
    applications, or any combination of them, made by any citizen or
    national of that foreign state or territory that are pending on the
    coming into force of the order or, if amended, the amendment, is to
    be suspended during the period or periods set out in the order or,
    if a period is not set out, during the period that the order is in
    force; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the processing of any of those types of
    applications, or any combination of them, made by any citizen or
    national of that foreign state or territory that are pending on the
    coming into force of the order or, if amended, the amendment, is to
    be terminated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other elements of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} restrict the application of the order to
    applications, citizens or nationals within a class of applications,
    citizens or nationals that is specified in the order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide for the retention, return or other
    disposition of applications;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the processing of applications is terminated,
    provide for the repayment of fees paid in respect of those
    applications; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} provide for any other matter arising out of or
    ancillary to the exercise of the power conferred under that
    subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The fact that an application is retained, returned or
  otherwise disposed of does not constitute a decision not to issue the
  visa or permit in relation to which the application is made.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 29, s. 304](#1156668-1174033){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156668-1174033.html"
  role="button"}
:::

#### [Federal Skilled Workers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275393 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application made before February 27, 2008

- **[[87.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application by a foreign national for a permanent resident visa as a
  member of the prescribed class of federal skilled workers that was
  made before February 27, 2008 is terminated if, before March 29, 2012,
  it has not been established by an officer, in accordance with the
  regulations, whether the applicant meets the selection criteria and
  other requirements applicable to that class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to an application in
  respect of which a superior court has made a final determination
  unless the determination is made on or after March 29, 2012.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The fact that an application is terminated under
  subsection (1) does not constitute a decision not to issue a permanent
  resident visa.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees returned

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any fees paid to the Minister in respect of the
  application referred to in subsection (1) --- including for the
  acquisition of permanent resident status --- must be returned, without
  interest, to the person who paid them. The amounts payable may be paid
  out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No recourse or indemnity

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No person has a right of recourse or indemnity
  against Her Majesty in connection with an application that is
  terminated under subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 19, s. 707
:::

#### [Federal Investor and Entrepreneur Classes]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275407 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pending applications

- **[[87.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87.5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  application by a foreign national for a permanent resident visa as a
  member of the prescribed class of investors or of entrepreneurs is
  terminated if, before February 11, 2014, it has not been established
  by an officer, in accordance with the regulations, whether the
  applicant meets the selection criteria and other requirements
  applicable to the class in question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an application in respect of which a superior court
    has made a final determination unless the determination is made on
    or after February 11, 2014; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an application made by an investor or entrepreneur
    who is selected as such by a province whose government has entered
    into an agreement referred to in subsection 9(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The fact that an application is terminated under
  subsection (1) does not constitute a decision not to issue a permanent
  resident visa.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees returned

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any fees paid to the Minister in respect of the
  application referred to in subsection (1) --- including for the
  acquisition of permanent resident status --- must be returned, without
  interest, to the person who paid them. The amounts payable may be paid
  out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investment returned

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If an application for a permanent resident visa as a
  member of the prescribed class of investors is terminated under
  subsection (1), an amount equal to the investment made by the
  applicant in respect of their application must be returned, without
  interest, to the applicant. The amount may be paid out of the
  Consolidated Revenue Fund.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial allocation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the provincial allocation of an investment made in
  respect of an application for a permanent resident visa as a member of
  the prescribed class of investors that is terminated under
  subsection (1) has been transferred to an approved fund, as defined in
  subsection 88(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2002-227), the province whose
  government controls the approved fund must return an amount equal to
  that provincial allocation to the Minister without delay. The return
  of the amount extinguishes the debt obligation in respect of that
  provincial allocation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No recourse or indemnity

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} No right of recourse or indemnity lies against Her
  Majesty in right of Canada in connection with an application that is
  terminated under subsection (1), including in respect of any contract
  or other arrangement relating to any aspect of the application.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 20, s. 303
:::

#### [Loans]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275427 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loans

- **[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister of Finance may, from time to time, advance to the Minister
  out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund, up to the maximum amount that is
  prescribed, sums that the Minister may require in order to make loans
  for the purposes of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may provide for any matter relating
  to the application of this section, and may include provisions
  respecting classes of persons to whom, and the purposes for which, the
  loans may be made.

#### [Fees]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275433 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  regulations may govern fees for services provided in the
  administration of this Act, and cases in which fees may be waived by
  the Minister or otherwise, individually or by class.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not
  apply to a fee for the provision of services in relation to a request
  for an assessment provided by the Department of Employment and Social
  Development with respect to an application for a work permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not
  apply to a fee for the provision of services in relation to the
  processing of an application

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for a temporary resident visa or permanent resident
    visa;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for a work permit or study permit;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for an extension of an authorization to remain in
    Canada as a temporary resident;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to remain in Canada as a permanent resident;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} to sponsor a foreign national as a member of the
    family class;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} to make the request referred to in subsection
    25(1);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} for a travel document issued under subsection
    31(3); and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} for a permanent resident card.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not
  apply to a fee for the provision of services in relation to an
  application referred to in subsection 11(1.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not
  apply to a fee for the provision of services in relation to the
  collection, use and disclosure of biometric information and for the
  provision of related services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not
  apply to a fee for the examination of the circumstances of a foreign
  national under subsection 25.2(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 89
- 2012, c. 17, s. 30, c. 31, ss. 312, 313
- 2013, c. 33, s. 162, c. 40, s. 237
- 2014, c. 39, s. 310
- 2017, c. 20, ss. 304, 454
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-89-20150612.html "Link to previous version of section 89"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees for rights and privileges

- **[[89.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} govern fees to be paid for rights and privileges
    conferred by means of a work permit; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} waive the fees referred to in paragraph (a) for
    certain work permits or certain classes of work permits.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not
  apply to fees referred to in paragraph (1)(a).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2013, c. 33, s. 163
- 2017, c. 20, s. 454
:::

[Previous
Version](section-89.1-20130626.html "Link to previous version of section 89.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

**[[89.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89.11 .sectionLabel}** The [Service Fees
Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not apply to fees for the acquisition of
permanent resident status.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 20, s. 305
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fees --- compliance regime

- **[[89.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} govern fees to be paid in respect of the compliance
    regime that applies to employers in relation to their employment of
    foreign nationals whose authorizations to work in Canada do not
    require an assessment provided by the Department of Employment and
    Social Development; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} govern cases in which the fees referred to in
    paragraph (a) are waived.

  - [(c) and (d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2015, c. 36, s.
    173\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The [Service Fees Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not
  apply to fees referred to in paragraph (1)(a).

::: HistoricalNote

- 2015, c. 36, s. 173
- 2017, c. 20, s. 454
:::

#### [Social Insurance Numbers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275480 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister directs special numbers to be
issued

**[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may direct
the Canada Employment Insurance Commission to assign to persons, other
than Canadian citizens or permanent residents, Social Insurance Numbers
identifying those persons as persons who may be required under this Act
to obtain authorization to work in Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 90
- 2012, c. 19, s. 311
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-90-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 90"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Representation or Advice]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275485 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representation or advice for
consideration

- **[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section, no person
  shall knowingly, directly or indirectly, represent or advise a person
  for consideration --- or offer to do so --- in connection with the
  submission of an expression of interest under subsection 10.1(3) or a
  proceeding or application under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons who may represent or advise

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person does not contravene subsection (1) if they
  are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a lawyer who is a member in good standing of a law
    society of a province or a notary who is a member in good standing
    of the [Chambre des notaires du Québec]{.otherLang lang="fr"};

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other member in good standing of a law society
    of a province or the [Chambre des notaires du Québec]{.otherLang
    lang="fr"}, including a paralegal; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a member in good standing of the
    [[College]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 2 of the
    [College of Immigration and Citizenship Consultants
    Act](/eng/acts/C-33.6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Students-at-law

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A student-at-law does not contravene subsection (1)
  by offering or providing representation or advice to a person if the
  student-at-law is acting under the supervision of a person mentioned
  in paragraph (2)(a) who is representing or advising the person --- or
  offering to do so --- in connection with the submission of an
  expression of interest under subsection 10.1(3) or a proceeding or
  application under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement or arrangement with Her
  Majesty

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An entity, including a person acting on its behalf,
  that offers or provides services to assist persons in connection with
  the submission of an expression of interest under subsection 10.1(3)
  or an application under this Act, including for a permanent or
  temporary resident visa, travel documents or a work or study permit,
  does not contravene subsection (1) if it is acting in accordance with
  an agreement or arrangement between that entity and Her Majesty in
  right of Canada that authorizes it to provide those services.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 29, s. 296\]]{.Repealed}

- [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 29, s. 296\]]{.Repealed}

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 29, s. 296\]]{.Repealed}

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 29, s. 296\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Québec Immigration Act

  [(7.1)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the Québec Immigration Act,
  CQLR, c. I-0.2.1, applies to, among other persons, every person who,
  in Quebec, represents or advises a person for consideration --- or
  offers to do so --- in connection with a proceeding or application
  under this Act and is a member of the
  [[College]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 2 of the
  [College of Immigration and Citizenship Consultants
  Act](/eng/acts/C-33.6).

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2019, c. 29, s. 296\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalties

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes subsection (1) commits
  an offence and is liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine of not more
    than \$200,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than two
    years, or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine of not more than
    \$40,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than six months,
    or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of
  [[proceeding]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, in this section,
  [[proceeding]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} does not include a proceeding
  before a superior court.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 91
- 2011, c. 8, s. 1
- 2013, c. 40, s. 292
- [2019, c. 29, s. 296](#1156614-1173889){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156614-1173889.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-91-20190621.html "Link to previous version of section 91"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[91.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish a system of administrative penalties and
    consequences --- including of administrative monetary penalties ---
    applicable to the violations designated in regulations made under
    paragraph (b) and set the amounts of those administrative monetary
    penalties;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designate as a violation the contravention ---
    including a contravention committed outside Canada --- of any
    specified provision of this Act or of the regulations by any person
    who, directly or indirectly, represents or advises a person for
    consideration --- or offers to do so --- in connection with the
    submission of an expression of interest under subsection 10.1(3) or
    a proceeding or application under this Act;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prohibit acts in relation to the activity of
    representing or advising --- or offering to do so --- described in
    paragraph (b); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} provide for the power to inspect --- including the
    power to require documents to be provided by individuals and
    entities for inspection --- for the purpose of verifying compliance
    with the provisions specified in regulations made under paragraph
    (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to request review

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any regulation made under paragraph (1)(a) must
  provide that a person who is the subject of a notice of violation has
  the right to request, from a person appointed under subsection (3), a
  review of the notice or of the penalty imposed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment --- order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, by order, appoint one or
  more Canadian citizens or permanent residents to conduct reviews in
  respect of notices of violation issued, or penalties imposed, under a
  regulation made under paragraph (1)(a) and to perform any other
  function conferred on them by a regulation made under that paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tenure

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person appointed by order under subsection (3)
  holds office during good behaviour for a term that the Governor in
  Council may specify, by order, but may be removed for cause by the
  Governor in Council at any time.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 29, s. 297](#1156630-1173904){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156630-1173904.html"
  role="button"}

  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410084-1414348.html"
  role="button"}

#### [Material Incorporated in Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275522 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporated material

- **[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  regulation may incorporate by reference the following material:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} material produced by a person or body other than
    the Governor in Council;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} material referred to in paragraph (a) that has been
    subsequently adapted or edited in order to facilitate its
    incorporation for the purposes of the regulation;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} material that has been developed jointly with
    another government or government agency for the purpose of
    harmonizing the regulation with other laws; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} material that is technical or explanatory in
    nature, such as specifications, classifications, illustrations or
    graphs, as well as examples that may assist in the application of
    the regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporated material --- instructions

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} An instruction given by the Minister or the
  Minister of Employment and Social Development under this Act may
  incorporate by reference any material, regardless of its source.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amended from time to time

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Material may be incorporated by reference on a
  specified date or as amended from time to time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporated material is not a
  regulation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, material that is incorporated
  by reference in a regulation made under this Act is not a regulation
  for the purposes of the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 92
- 2012, c. 19, s. 708
- 2013, c. 33, s. 164, c. 40, s. 238
- 2015, c. 3, s. 113(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-92-20131212.html "Link to previous version of section 92"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

**[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** Instructions given by the
Minister or the Minister of Employment and Social Development under this
Act and guidelines issued by the Chairperson under paragraph 159(1)(h)
are not statutory instruments for the purposes of the [Statutory
Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 93
- 2013, c. 33, s. 165, c. 40, s. 238
:::

[Previous
Version](section-93-20130626.html "Link to previous version of section 93"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Report to Parliament]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275542 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report to Parliament

- **[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister must, on or before November 1 of each year or, if a House of
  Parliament is not then sitting, within the next 30 days on which that
  House is sitting after that date, table in each House of Parliament a
  report on the operation of this Act in the preceding calendar year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The report shall include a description of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the instructions given under section 87.3 and other
    activities and initiatives taken concerning the selection of foreign
    nationals, including measures taken in cooperation with the
    provinces;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} any instructions given under paragraph
    10.3(1)(h.2) that establish a category of eligible foreign
    nationals, the economic goal sought to be supported in establishing
    the category and the number of foreign nationals invited to make an
    application for permanent residence in respect of the category;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of Canada, the number of foreign
    nationals who became permanent residents, and the number projected
    to become permanent residents in the following year;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} in respect of Canada, the linguistic profile of
    foreign nationals who became permanent residents;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of each province that has entered into a
    federal-provincial agreement described in subsection 9(1), the
    number, for each class listed in the agreement, of persons that
    became permanent residents and that the province projects will
    become permanent residents there in the following year;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the number of temporary resident permits issued
    under section 24, categorized according to grounds of
    inadmissibility, if any;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the number of persons granted permanent resident
    status under each of subsections 25(1), 25.1(1) and 25.2(1);

  - [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} any instructions given under subsection 30(1.2),
    (1.41) or (1.43) during the year in question and the date of their
    publication; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a gender-based analysis of the impact of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 94
- 2008, c. 28, s. 119
- 2010, c. 8, s. 9
- 2012, c. 1, s. 207
- 2013, c. 33, s. 166
- [2022, c. 10, s. 379](#1357793-1367049){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1357793-1367049.html"
  role="button"}
:::

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Refugee Protection]{.HTitleText1} {#h-275558 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Refugee Protection, Convention Refugees and Persons in Need of Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275559 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conferral of refugee protection

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Refugee protection is conferred on a
  person when

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person has been determined to be a Convention
    refugee or a person in similar circumstances under a visa
    application and becomes a permanent resident under the visa or a
    temporary resident under a temporary resident permit for protection
    reasons;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Board determines the person to be a Convention
    refugee or a person in need of protection; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} except in the case of a person described in
    subsection 112(3), the Minister allows an application for
    protection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protected person

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A protected person is a person on whom refugee
  protection is conferred under subsection (1), and whose claim or
  application has not subsequently been deemed to be rejected under
  subsection 108(3), 109(3) or 114(4).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 95
- 2010, c. 8, s. 10(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-95-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 95"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convention refugee

**[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** A Convention refugee is a
person who, by reason of a well-founded fear of persecution for reasons
of race, religion, nationality, membership in a particular social group
or political opinion,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is outside each of their countries of nationality and
  is unable or, by reason of that fear, unwilling to avail themself of
  the protection of each of those countries; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} not having a country of nationality, is outside the
  country of their former habitual residence and is unable or, by reason
  of that fear, unwilling to return to that country.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person in need of protection

- **[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person in need of protection is a person in Canada whose removal to
  their country or countries of nationality or, if they do not have a
  country of nationality, their country of former habitual residence,
  would subject them personally

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to a danger, believed on substantial grounds to
    exist, of torture within the meaning of Article 1 of the Convention
    Against Torture; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to a risk to their life or to a risk of cruel and
    unusual treatment or punishment if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person is unable or, because of that risk,
      unwilling to avail themself of the protection of that country,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the risk would be faced by the person in every
      part of that country and is not faced generally by other
      individuals in or from that country,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the risk is not inherent or incidental to
      lawful sanctions, unless imposed in disregard of accepted
      international standards, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the risk is not caused by the inability of that
      country to provide adequate health or medical care.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person in need of protection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person in Canada who is a member of a class of
  persons prescribed by the regulations as being in need of protection
  is also a person in need of protection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion --- Refugee Convention

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** A person referred to in
section E or F of Article 1 of the Refugee Convention is not a
Convention refugee or a person in need of protection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to report

- **[[98.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  designated foreign national on whom refugee protection is conferred
  under paragraph 95(1)(b) or (c) must report to an officer in
  accordance with the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation when reporting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A designated foreign national who is required to
  report to an officer must answer truthfully all questions put to him
  or her and must provide any information and documents that the officer
  requests.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 17, s. 32
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[98.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98.2 .sectionLabel}** The regulations may
provide for any matter relating to the application of section 98.1 and
may include provisions respecting the requirement to report to an
officer.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 17, s. 32
:::

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Convention Refugees and Persons in Need of Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275598 .Subheading}

#### [Claim for Refugee Protection]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275599 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Claim

- **[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  claim for refugee protection may be made in or outside Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Claim outside Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A claim for refugee protection made by a person
  outside Canada must be made by making an application for a visa as a
  Convention refugee or a person in similar circumstances, and is
  governed by Part 1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Claim inside Canada

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A claim for refugee protection made by a person
  inside Canada must be made in person to an officer, must not be made
  by a person who is subject to a removal order, and is governed by this
  Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Claim made inside Canada --- not at
  port of entry

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} A person who makes a claim for refugee protection
  inside Canada other than at a port of entry must provide the officer,
  within the time limits provided for in the regulations, with the
  documents and information --- including in respect of the basis for
  the claim --- required by the rules of the Board, in accordance with
  those rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permanent resident

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An application to become a permanent resident made by
  a protected person is governed by Part 1.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 99
- 2012, c. 17, s. 33
- [2023, c. 26, s. 284](#1410034-1414345){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1410034-1414345.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-99-20121215.html "Link to previous version of section 99"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Examination of Eligibility to Refer Claim]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275613 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral to Refugee Protection Division

- **[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer shall, after receipt of a claim referred to in subsection
  99(3), determine whether the claim is eligible to be referred to the
  Refugee Protection Division and, if it is eligible, shall refer the
  claim in accordance with the rules of the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The burden of proving that a claim is eligible to
  be referred to the Refugee Protection Division rests on the claimant,
  who must answer truthfully all questions put to them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The officer shall suspend consideration of the
  eligibility of the person's claim if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a report has been referred for a determination, at
    an admissibility hearing, of whether the person is inadmissible on
    grounds of security, violating human or international rights,
    serious criminality or organized criminality; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the officer considers it necessary to wait for a
    decision of a court with respect to a claimant who is charged with
    an offence under an Act of Parliament that is punishable by a
    maximum term of imprisonment of at least 10 years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of claim

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Refugee Protection Division may not consider a
  claim until it is referred by the officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents and information to be
  provided

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person who makes a claim for refugee protection
  inside Canada at a port of entry and whose claim is referred to the
  Refugee Protection Division must provide the Division, within the time
  limits provided for in the regulations, with the documents and
  information --- including in respect of the basis for the claim ---
  required by the rules of the Board, in accordance with those rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date of hearing

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} The referring officer must, in accordance with the
  regulations, the rules of the Board and any directions of the
  Chairperson of the Board, fix the date on which the claimant is to
  attend a hearing before the Refugee Protection Division.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1)

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a traveller is detained or isolated under the
  [Quarantine Act](/eng/acts/Q-1.1), the period referred to in
  subsections (1) and (3) does not begin to run until the day on which
  the detention or isolation ends.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 100
- 2005, c. 20, s. 81
- 2010, c. 8, s. 11
- 2012, c. 17, s. 56
- [2019, c. 29, s. 305](#1156670-1173946){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156670-1173946.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 32, s. 58(F)](#1433217-1433941){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433217-1433941.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-100-20190621.html "Link to previous version of section 100"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ineligibility

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  claim is ineligible to be referred to the Refugee Protection Division
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} refugee protection has been conferred on the
    claimant under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a claim for refugee protection by the claimant has
    been rejected by the Board;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a prior claim by the claimant was determined to be
    ineligible to be referred to the Refugee Protection Division, or to
    have been withdrawn or abandoned;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} the claimant has, before making a claim for
    refugee protection in Canada, made a claim for refugee protection to
    a country other than Canada, and the fact of its having been made
    has been confirmed in accordance with an agreement or arrangement
    entered into by Canada and that country for the purpose of
    facilitating information sharing to assist in the administration and
    enforcement of their immigration and citizenship laws;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the claimant has been recognized as a Convention
    refugee by a country other than Canada and can be sent or returned
    to that country;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the claimant came directly or indirectly to Canada
    from a country designated by the regulations, other than a country
    of their nationality or their former habitual residence; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the claimant has been determined to be inadmissible
    on grounds of security, violating human or international rights,
    serious criminality or organized criminality.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Serious criminality

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A claim is not ineligible by reason of serious
  criminality under paragraph (1)(f) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of inadmissibility by reason of a
    conviction in Canada, the conviction is for an offence under an Act
    of Parliament punishable by a maximum term of imprisonment of at
    least 10 years; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of inadmissibility by reason of a
    conviction outside Canada, the conviction is for an offence that, if
    committed in Canada, would constitute an offence under an Act of
    Parliament punishable by a maximum term of imprisonment of at least
    10 years.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 101
- 2012, c. 17, s. 34
- [2019, c. 29, s. 306](#1156675-1173948){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156675-1173948.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 19, s. 12](#1411918-1414330){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1411918-1414330.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 32, s. 59(F)](#1433219-1433942){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433219-1433942.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-101-20230622.html "Link to previous version of section 101"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  regulations may govern matters relating to the application of sections
  100 and 101, may, for the purposes of this Act, define the terms used
  in those sections and, for the purpose of sharing responsibility with
  governments of foreign states for the consideration of refugee claims,
  may include provisions

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} designating countries that comply with Article 33
    of the Refugee Convention and Article 3 of the Convention Against
    Torture;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} making a list of those countries and amending it as
    necessary; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the circumstances and criteria for the
    application of paragraph 101(1)(e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following factors are to be considered in
  designating a country under paragraph (1)(a):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether the country is a party to the Refugee
    Convention and to the Convention Against Torture;
