  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} its policies and practices with respect to claims
    under the Refugee Convention and with respect to obligations under
    the Convention Against Torture;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} its human rights record; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} whether it is party to an agreement with the
    Government of Canada for the purpose of sharing responsibility with
    respect to claims for refugee protection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council must ensure the continuing
  review of factors set out in subsection (2) with respect to each
  designated country.

#### [Suspension or Termination of Consideration of Claim]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275662 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension

- **[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Proceedings of the Refugee
  Protection Division in respect of a claim for refugee protection are
  suspended on notice by an officer that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the matter has been referred to the Immigration
    Division to determine whether the claimant is inadmissible on
    grounds of security, violating human or international rights,
    serious criminality or organized criminality; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an officer considers it necessary to wait for a
    decision of a court with respect to a claimant who is charged with
    an offence under an Act of Parliament that may be punished by a
    maximum term of imprisonment of at least 10 years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On notice by an officer that the suspended claim was
  determined to be eligible, proceedings of the Refugee Protection
  Division must continue.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 103
- 2012, c. 17, s. 35
- [2023, c. 32, s. 60(F)](#1433221-1433943){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433221-1433943.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-103-20121215.html "Link to previous version of section 103"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of ineligible claim

- **[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer may, with respect to a claim that is before the Refugee
  Protection Division or, in the case of paragraph (a.1) or (d), that is
  before or has been determined by the Refugee Protection Division or
  the Refugee Appeal Division, give notice that an officer has
  determined that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the claim is ineligible under paragraphs 101(1)(a)
    to (e), other than paragraph 101(1)(c.1);

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the claim is ineligible under paragraph
    101(1)(c.1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the claim is ineligible under paragraph 101(1)(f);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the claim was referred as a result of directly or
    indirectly misrepresenting or withholding material facts relating to
    a relevant matter and that the claim was not otherwise eligible to
    be referred to that Division; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the claim is not the first claim that was received
    by an officer in respect of the claimant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination and nullification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice given under the following provisions has the
  following effects:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if given under paragraph (1)(a), (b) or (c), it
    terminates pending proceedings in the Refugee Protection Division
    respecting the claim;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} if given under paragraph (1)(a.1), it terminates
    pending proceedings in the Refugee Protection Division or, in the
    case of an appeal made by the claimant, the Refugee Appeal Division,
    respecting the claim; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if given under paragraph (1)(d), it terminates
    proceedings in and nullifies any decision of the Refugee Protection
    Division or the Refugee Appeal Division respecting a claim other
    than the first claim.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 104
- [2019, c. 29, s. 307](#1156677-1173954){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156677-1173954.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-104-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 104"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Extradition Procedure]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275683 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension if proceeding under
[Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01)

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Refugee Protection Division and Refugee Appeal Division shall not
  commence, or shall suspend, consideration of any matter concerning a
  person against whom an authority to proceed has been issued under
  section 15 of the [Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01) with respect to
  an offence under Canadian law that is punishable under an Act of
  Parliament by a maximum term of imprisonment of at least 10 years,
  until a final decision under the [Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01)
  with respect to the discharge or surrender of the person has been
  made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation if discharge under
  [Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the person is finally discharged under the
  [Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01), the proceedings of the
  applicable Division may be commenced or continued as though there had
  not been any proceedings under that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rejection if surrender under
  [Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the person is ordered surrendered by the Minister
  of Justice under the [Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01) and the
  offence for which the person was committed by the judge under section
  29 of that Act is punishable under an Act of Parliament by a maximum
  term of imprisonment of at least 10 years, the order of surrender is
  deemed to be a rejection of a claim for refugee protection based on
  paragraph (b) of Section F of Article 1 of the Refugee Convention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Final decision

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The deemed rejection referred to in subsection (3)
  may not be appealed, and is not subject to judicial review except to
  the extent that a judicial review of the order of surrender is
  provided for under the [Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limit if no previous claim

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the person has not made a claim for refugee
  protection before the order of surrender referred to in subsection
  (3), the person may not do so before the surrender.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 105
- [2023, c. 32, s. 61(F)](#1433223-1433946){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433223-1433946.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-105-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 105"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Claimant Without Identification]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275695 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Credibility

**[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** The Refugee Protection
Division must take into account, with respect to the credibility of a
claimant, whether the claimant possesses acceptable documentation
establishing identity, and if not, whether they have provided a
reasonable explanation for the lack of documentation or have taken
reasonable steps to obtain the documentation.

#### [Decision on Claim for Refugee Protection]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275698 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Refugee Protection Division shall accept a claim for refugee
  protection if it determines that the claimant is a Convention refugee
  or person in need of protection, and shall otherwise reject the claim.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No credible basis

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Refugee Protection Division is of the opinion,
  in rejecting a claim, that there was no credible or trustworthy
  evidence on which it could have made a favourable decision, it shall
  state in its reasons for the decision that there is no credible basis
  for the claim.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manifestly unfounded

**[[107.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107.1 .sectionLabel}** If the Refugee
Protection Division rejects a claim for refugee protection, it must
state in its reasons for the decision that the claim is manifestly
unfounded if it is of the opinion that the claim is clearly fraudulent.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 8, s. 11.1
- 2012, c. 17, s. 57
:::

#### [Cessation of Refugee Protection]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275708 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rejection

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  claim for refugee protection shall be rejected, and a person is not a
  Convention refugee or a person in need of protection, in any of the
  following circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person has voluntarily reavailed themself of
    the protection of their country of nationality;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person has voluntarily reacquired their
    nationality;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person has acquired a new nationality and
    enjoys the protection of the country of that new nationality;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the person has voluntarily become re-established in
    the country that the person left or remained outside of and in
    respect of which the person claimed refugee protection in Canada; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the reasons for which the person sought refugee
    protection have ceased to exist.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of refugee protection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On application by the Minister, the Refugee
  Protection Division may determine that refugee protection referred to
  in subsection 95(1) has ceased for any of the reasons described in
  subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the application is allowed, the claim of the
  person is deemed to be rejected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(e) does not apply to a person who
  establishes that there are compelling reasons arising out of previous
  persecution, torture, treatment or punishment for refusing to avail
  themselves of the protection of the country which they left, or
  outside of which they remained, due to such previous persecution,
  torture, treatment or punishment.

#### [Applications to Vacate]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275723 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacation of refugee protection

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Refugee Protection Division may, on application by the Minister,
  vacate a decision to allow a claim for refugee protection, if it finds
  that the decision was obtained as a result of directly or indirectly
  misrepresenting or withholding material facts relating to a relevant
  matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rejection of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Refugee Protection Division may reject the
  application if it is satisfied that other sufficient evidence was
  considered at the time of the first determination to justify refugee
  protection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Allowance of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the application is allowed, the claim of the
  person is deemed to be rejected and the decision that led to the
  conferral of refugee protection is nullified.

#### [Designated Countries of Origin]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275731 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation of countries of origin

- **[[109.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by order,
  designate a country, for the purposes of subsection 110(2) and section
  111.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may only make a designation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case where the number of claims for refugee
    protection made in Canada by nationals of the country in question in
    respect of which the Refugee Protection Division has made a final
    determination is equal to or greater than the number provided for by
    order of the Minister,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if the rate, expressed as a percentage, that is
      obtained by dividing the total number of claims made by nationals
      of the country in question that, in a final determination by the
      Division during the period provided for in the order, are rejected
      or determined to be withdrawn or abandoned by the total number of
      claims made by nationals of the country in question in respect of
      which the Division has, during the same period, made a final
      determination is equal to or greater than the percentage provided
      for in the order, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the rate, expressed as a percentage, that is
      obtained by dividing the total number of claims made by nationals
      of the country in question that, in a final determination by the
      Division, during the period provided for in the order, are
      determined to be withdrawn or abandoned by the total number of
      claims made by nationals of the country in question in respect of
      which the Division has, during the same period, made a final
      determination is equal to or greater than the percentage provided
      for in the order; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case where the number of claims for refugee
    protection made in Canada by nationals of the country in question in
    respect of which the Refugee Protection Division has made a final
    determination is less than the number provided for by order of the
    Minister, if the Minister is of the opinion that in the country in
    question

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} there is an independent judicial system,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} basic democratic rights and freedoms are
      recognized and mechanisms for redress are available if those
      rights or freedoms are infringed, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} civil society organizations exist.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of Minister

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by order, provide for the number,
  period or percentages referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) or (3) is not a
  statutory instrument for the purposes of the [Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22). However, it must be published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 8, s. 12
- 2012, c. 17, s. 58
:::

#### [Appeal to Refugee Appeal Division]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275750 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

- **[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (1.1) and
  (2), a person or the Minister may appeal, in accordance with the rules
  of the Board, on a question of law, of fact or of mixed law and fact,
  to the Refugee Appeal Division against a decision of the Refugee
  Protection Division to allow or reject the person's claim for refugee
  protection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of appeal

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may satisfy any requirement respecting
  the manner in which an appeal is filed and perfected by submitting a
  notice of appeal and any supporting documents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction on appeals

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No appeal may be made in respect of any of the
  following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a decision of the Refugee Protection Division
    allowing or rejecting the claim for refugee protection of a
    designated foreign national;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a determination that a refugee protection claim has
    been withdrawn or abandoned;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a decision of the Refugee Protection Division
    rejecting a claim for refugee protection that states that the claim
    has no credible basis or is manifestly unfounded;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to the regulations, a decision of the
    Refugee Protection Division in respect of a claim for refugee
    protection if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the foreign national who makes the claim came
      directly or indirectly to Canada from a country that is, on the
      day on which their claim is made, designated by regulations made
      under subsection 102(1) and that is a party to an agreement
      referred to in paragraph 102(2)(d), and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the claim --- by virtue of regulations made
      under paragraph 102(1)(c) --- is not ineligible under paragraph
      101(1)(e) to be referred to the Refugee Protection Division;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} a decision of the Refugee Protection Division
    allowing or rejecting a claim for refugee protection made by a
    foreign national who is a national of a country that was, on the day
    on which the decision was made, a country designated under
    subsection 109.1(1);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a decision of the Refugee Protection Division
    allowing or rejecting an application by the Minister for a
    determination that refugee protection has ceased;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a decision of the Refugee Protection Division
    allowing or rejecting an application by the Minister to vacate a
    decision to allow a claim for refugee protection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Making of appeal

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The appeal must be filed and perfected within the
  time limits set out in the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3.1), (4) and (6), the
  Refugee Appeal Division must proceed without a hearing, on the basis
  of the record of the proceedings of the Refugee Protection Division,
  and may accept documentary evidence and written submissions from the
  Minister and the person who is the subject of the appeal and, in the
  case of a matter that is conducted before a panel of three members,
  written submissions from a representative or agent of the United
  Nations High Commissioner for Refugees and any other person described
  in the rules of the Board.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limits

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} Unless a hearing is held under subsection (6), the
  Refugee Appeal Division must make a decision within the time limits
  set out in the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence that may be presented

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On appeal, the person who is the subject of the
  appeal may present only evidence that arose after the rejection of
  their claim or that was not reasonably available, or that the person
  could not reasonably have been expected in the circumstances to have
  presented, at the time of the rejection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (4) does not apply in respect of evidence
  that is presented in response to evidence presented by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hearing

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Refugee Appeal Division may hold a hearing if, in
  its opinion, there is documentary evidence referred to in subsection
  (3)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that raises a serious issue with respect to the
    credibility of the person who is the subject of the appeal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is central to the decision with respect to the
    refugee protection claim; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that, if accepted, would justify allowing or
    rejecting the refugee protection claim.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 110
- 2010, c. 8, s. 13
- 2012, c. 17, ss. 36, 84
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-110-20120628.html "Link to previous version of section 110"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After considering the appeal, the
  Refugee Appeal Division shall make one of the following decisions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} confirm the determination of the Refugee Protection
    Division;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} set aside the determination and substitute a
    determination that, in its opinion, should have been made; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} refer the matter to the Refugee Protection Division
    for re-determination, giving the directions to the Refugee
    Protection Division that it considers appropriate.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 17, s. 37\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referrals

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Refugee Appeal Division may make the referral
  described in paragraph (1)(c) only if it is of the opinion that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the decision of the Refugee Protection Division is
    wrong in law, in fact or in mixed law and fact; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it cannot make a decision under paragraph 111(1)(a)
    or (b) without hearing evidence that was presented to the Refugee
    Protection Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 111
- 2010, c. 8, s. 14
- 2012, c. 17, s. 37
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-111-20120628.html "Link to previous version of section 111"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275797 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[111.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may provide for any
  matter relating to the application of this Division, and may include
  provisions respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} time limits for the provision of documents and
    information under subsection 99(3.1) or 100(4);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} time limits for the hearing referred to in
    subsection 100(4.1);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} exceptions to the application of paragraph
    110(2)(d);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} time limits for the filing and perfecting of an
    appeal under subsection 110(2.1); and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} time limits for the making of a decision by the
    Refugee Appeal Division, the extension of those time limits and the
    circumstances in which they do not apply.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification --- regulations made
  under paragraph (1)(b)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} With respect to claimants who are nationals of a
  country that is, on the day on which their claim is made, a country
  designated under subsection 109.1(1), regulations made under paragraph
  (1)(b) may provide for time limits that are different from the time
  limits for other claimants.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 8, s. 14.1
- 2012, c. 17, s. 59
:::

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Pre-removal Risk Assessment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275810 .Subheading}

#### [Protection]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275811 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for protection

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person in Canada, other than a person referred to in subsection
  115(1), may, in accordance with the regulations, apply to the Minister
  for protection if they are subject to a removal order that is in force
  or are named in a certificate described in subsection 77(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a person may not apply for
  protection if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are the subject of an authority to proceed
    issued under section 15 of the [Extradition Act](/eng/acts/E-23.01);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have made a claim to refugee protection that
    has been determined under paragraph 101(1)(e) to be ineligible;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2.1), less than 12 months,
    or, in the case of a person who is a national of a country that is
    designated under subsection 109.1(1), less than 36 months, have
    passed since

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the day on which their claim for refugee
      protection was rejected --- unless it was deemed to be rejected
      under subsection 109(3) or was rejected on the basis of section E
      or F of Article 1 of the Refugee Convention --- or determined to
      be withdrawn or abandoned by the Refugee Protection Division, in
      the case where no appeal was made and no application was made to
      the Federal Court for leave to commence an application for
      judicial review, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the latest of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the day on which their claim for refugee
        protection was rejected --- unless it was deemed to be rejected
        under subsection 109(3) or was rejected on the basis of section
        E or F of Article 1 of the Refugee Convention --- or determined
        to be withdrawn or abandoned by the Refugee Protection Division
        or, if there was more than one such rejection or determination,
        the day on which the last one occurred,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the day on which their claim for refugee
        protection was rejected --- unless it was rejected on the basis
        of section E or F of Article 1 of the Refugee Convention --- or
        determined to be withdrawn or abandoned by the Refugee Appeal
        Division or, if there was more than one such rejection or
        determination, the day on which the last one occurred, and

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the Federal Court refused
        their application for leave to commence an application for
        judicial review, or denied their application for judicial
        review, with respect to their claim for refugee protection,
        unless that claim was deemed to be rejected under subsection
        109(3) or was rejected on the basis of section E or F of Article
        1 of the Refugee Convention; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2.1), less than 12 months,
    or, in the case of a person who is a national of a country that is
    designated under subsection 109.1(1), less than 36 months, have
    passed since

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the day on which their application for protection
      was rejected or determined to be withdrawn or abandoned by the
      Minister, in the case where no application was made to the Federal
      Court for leave to commence an application for judicial review, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the later of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the day on which their application for
        protection was rejected or determined to be withdrawn or
        abandoned by the Minister or, if there was more than one such
        rejection or determination, the day on which the last one
        occurred, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the Federal Court refused
        their application for leave to commence an application for
        judicial review, or denied their application for judicial
        review, with respect to their application for protection.

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 17, s. 38\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may exempt from the application of
  paragraph (2)(b.1) or (c)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nationals --- or, in the case of persons who do
    not have a country of nationality, the former habitual residents ---
    of a country;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the nationals or former habitual residents of a
    country who, before they left the country, lived in a given part of
    that country; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a class of nationals or former habitual residents
    of a country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} However, an exemption made under subsection (2.1)
  does not apply to persons in respect of whom, after the day on which
  the exemption comes into force, a decision is made respecting their
  claim for refugee protection by the Refugee Protection Division or, if
  an appeal is made, by the Refugee Appeal Division.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may govern any matter relating to
  the application of subsection (2.1) or (2.2) and may include
  provisions establishing the criteria to be considered when an
  exemption is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Refugee protection may not be conferred on an
  applicant who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is determined to be inadmissible on grounds of
    security, violating human or international rights or organized
    criminality;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is determined to be inadmissible on grounds of
    serious criminality with respect to a conviction in Canada of an
    offence under an Act of Parliament punishable by a maximum term of
    imprisonment of at least 10 years or with respect to a conviction
    outside Canada for an offence that, if committed in Canada, would
    constitute an offence under an Act of Parliament punishable by a
    maximum term of imprisonment of at least 10 years;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} made a claim to refugee protection that was
    rejected on the basis of section F of Article 1 of the Refugee
    Convention; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} is named in a certificate referred to in subsection
    77(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 112
- 2010, c. 8, s. 15
- 2012, c. 17, ss. 38, 60, 84
- 2015, c. 3, s. 114(E)
- [2019, c. 29, s. 308](#1156682-1173968){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156682-1173968.html"
  role="button"}
- [2023, c. 32, s. 62(F)](#1433225-1433947){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433225-1433947.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-112-20190621.html "Link to previous version of section 112"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of application

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** Consideration of an
application for protection shall be as follows:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an applicant whose claim to refugee protection has
  been rejected may present only new evidence that arose after the
  rejection or was not reasonably available, or that the applicant could
  not reasonably have been expected in the circumstances to have
  presented, at the time of the rejection;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a hearing may be held if the Minister, on the basis
  of prescribed factors, is of the opinion that a hearing is required;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an applicant not described in
  subsection 112(3), consideration shall be on the basis of sections 96
  to 98;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an applicant described in subsection
  112(3) --- other than one described in subparagraph (e)(i) or (ii) ---
  consideration shall be on the basis of the factors set out in section
  97 and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an applicant for protection who is
    inadmissible on grounds of serious criminality, whether they are a
    danger to the public in Canada, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any other applicant, whether the
    application should be refused because of the nature and severity of
    acts committed by the applicant or because of the danger that the
    applicant constitutes to the security of Canada; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the following applicants,
  consideration shall be on the basis of sections 96 to 98 and
  subparagraph (d)(i) or (ii), as the case may be:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an applicant who is determined to be inadmissible
    on grounds of serious criminality with respect to a conviction in
    Canada of an offence under an Act of Parliament punishable by a
    maximum term of imprisonment of at least 10 years for which a term
    of imprisonment of less than two years --- or no term of
    imprisonment --- was imposed, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an applicant who is determined to be inadmissible
    on grounds of serious criminality with respect to a conviction of an
    offence outside Canada that, if committed in Canada, would
    constitute an offence under an Act of Parliament punishable by a
    maximum term of imprisonment of at least 10 years, unless they are
    found to be a person referred to in section F of Article 1 of the
    Refugee Convention.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 113
- 2012, c. 17, s. 39
- [2023, c. 32, s. 63](#1433227-1433949){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1433227-1433949.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-113-20121215.html "Link to previous version of section 113"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory hearing

**[[113.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113.01 .sectionLabel}** Unless the
application is allowed without a hearing, a hearing must, despite
paragraph 113(b), be held in the case of an applicant for protection
whose claim for refugee protection has been determined to be ineligible
solely under paragraph 101(1)(c.1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2019, c. 29, s. 308.1](#1156691-1173972){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1156691-1173972.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of decision

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  decision to allow the application for protection has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an applicant not described in
    subsection 112(3), the effect of conferring refugee protection; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an applicant described in subsection
    112(3), the effect of staying the removal order with respect to a
    country or place in respect of which the applicant was determined to
    be in need of protection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation of stay

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is of the opinion that the
  circumstances surrounding a stay of the enforcement of a removal order
  have changed, the Minister may re-examine, in accordance with
  paragraph 113(d) and the regulations, the grounds on which the
  application was allowed and may cancel the stay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vacation of determination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is of the opinion that a decision to
  allow an application for protection was obtained as a result of
  directly or indirectly misrepresenting or withholding material facts
  on a relevant matter, the Minister may vacate the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of vacation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a decision is vacated under subsection (3), it is
  nullified and the application for protection is deemed to have been
  rejected.

#### [Principle of Non-refoulement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-275863 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  protected person or a person who is recognized as a Convention refugee
  by another country to which the person may be returned shall not be
  removed from Canada to a country where they would be at risk of
  persecution for reasons of race, religion, nationality, membership in
  a particular social group or political opinion or at risk of torture
  or cruel and unusual treatment or punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in the case of a person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} who is inadmissible on grounds of serious
    criminality and who constitutes, in the opinion of the Minister, a
    danger to the public in Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} who is inadmissible on grounds of security,
    violating human or international rights or organized criminality if,
    in the opinion of the Minister, the person should not be allowed to
    remain in Canada on the basis of the nature and severity of acts
    committed or of danger to the security of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Removal of refugee

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person, after a determination under paragraph
  101(1)(e) that the person's claim is ineligible, is to be sent to the
  country from which the person came to Canada, but may be sent to
  another country if that country is designated under subsection 102(1)
  or if the country from which the person came to Canada has rejected
  their claim for refugee protection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** The regulations may
provide for any matter relating to the application of this Division, and
may include provisions respecting procedures to be followed with respect
to applications for protection and decisions made under section 115,
including the establishment of factors to determine whether a hearing is
required.

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Enforcement]{.HTitleText1} {#h-275875 .Part}

### [Human Smuggling and Trafficking]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275876 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Organizing entry into Canada

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall organize, induce, aid or abet the coming into Canada of
  one or more persons knowing that, or being reckless as to whether,
  their coming into Canada is or would be in contravention of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalties --- fewer than 10 persons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who contravenes subsection (1) with respect
  to fewer than 10 persons is guilty of an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine of not more than
      \$500,000 or to a term of imprisonment of not more than 10 years,
      or to both, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a subsequent offence, to a fine of not more
      than \$1,000,000 or to a term of imprisonment of not more than 14
      years, or to both; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine of not more than
    \$100,000 or to a term of imprisonment of not more than two years,
    or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty --- 10 persons or more

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who contravenes subsection (1) with respect
  to a group of 10 persons or more is guilty of an offence and liable on
  conviction by way of indictment to a fine of not more than \$1,000,000
  or to life imprisonment, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum penalty --- fewer than 50
  persons

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} A person who is convicted on indictment of an
  offence under subsection (2) or (3) with respect to fewer than 50
  persons is also liable to a minimum punishment of imprisonment for a
  term of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} three years, if either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person, in committing the offence, endangered
      the life or safety of, or caused bodily harm or death to, any of
      the persons with respect to whom the offence was committed, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the commission of the offence was for profit, or
      was for the benefit of, at the direction of or in association with
      a criminal organization or terrorist group; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} five years, if both

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person, in committing the offence, endangered
      the life or safety of, or caused bodily harm or death to, any of
      the persons with respect to whom the offence was committed, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the commission of the offence was for profit, or
      was for the benefit of, at the direction of or in association with
      a criminal organization or terrorist group.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minimum penalty --- 50 persons or more

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} A person who is convicted of an offence under
  subsection (3) with respect to a group of 50 persons or more is also
  liable to a minimum punishment of imprisonment for a term of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} five years, if either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person, in committing the offence, endangered
      the life or safety of, or caused bodily harm or death to, any of
      the persons with respect to whom the offence was committed, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the commission of the offence was for profit, or
      was for the benefit of, at the direction of or in association with
      a criminal organization or terrorist group; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 10 years, if both

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person, in committing the offence, endangered
      the life or safety of, or caused bodily harm or death to, any of
      the persons with respect to whom the offence was committed, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the commission of the offence was for profit, or
      was for the benefit of, at the direction of or in association with
      a criminal organization or terrorist group.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No proceedings without consent

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No proceedings for an offence under this section may
  be instituted except by or with the consent of the Attorney General of
  Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 117
- 2012, c. 17, s. 41
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-117-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 117"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence --- trafficking in persons

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall knowingly organize the coming into Canada of one or more
  persons by means of abduction, fraud, deception or use or threat of
  force or coercion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[organize]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1),
  [[organize]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, with respect to persons, includes
  their recruitment or transportation and, after their entry into
  Canada, the receipt or harbouring of those persons.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disembarking persons at sea

**[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** A person shall not
disembark a person or group of persons at sea for the purpose of
inducing, aiding or abetting them to come into Canada in contravention
of this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalties

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** A person who
contravenes section 118 or 119 is guilty of an offence and liable on
conviction by way of indictment to a fine of not more than \$1,000,000
or to life imprisonment, or to both.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggravating factors

**[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** The court, in
determining the penalty to be imposed under section 120, shall take into
account whether

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} bodily harm or death occurred, or the life or safety
  of any person was endangered, as a result of the commission of the
  offence;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the commission of the offence was for the benefit of,
  at the direction of or in association with a criminal organization;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the commission of the offence was for profit, whether
  or not any profit was realized; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person was subjected to humiliating or degrading
  treatment, including with respect to work or health conditions or
  sexual exploitation as a result of the commission of the offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 121
- 2012, c. 17, s. 42
- 2017, c. 26, s. 44
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-121-20121215.html "Link to previous version of section 121"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[criminal
organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[121.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subparagraphs
  117(3.1)(a)(ii) and (b)(ii) and (3.2)(a)(ii) and (b)(ii) and paragraph
  121(b), [[criminal organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a criminal
  organization as defined in subsection 467.1(1) of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[terrorist
  group]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subparagraphs 117(3.1)(a)(ii) and
  (b)(ii) and (3.2)(a)(ii) and (b)(ii), [[terrorist
  group]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a terrorist group as defined in
  subsection 83.01(1) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 17, s. 43
:::

### [Offences Related to Documents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275932 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Documents

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall, in order to contravene this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} possess a passport, visa or other document, of
    Canadian or foreign origin, that purports to establish or that could
    be used to establish a person's identity;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} use such a document, including for the purpose of
    entering or remaining in Canada; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} import, export or deal in such a document.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of offence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Proof of the matters referred to in subsection (1) in
  relation to a forged document or a document that is blank, incomplete,
  altered or not genuine is, in the absence of evidence to the contrary,
  proof that the person intends to contravene this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalty

- **[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 122(1)(a) is guilty of an offence and
    liable on conviction on indictment to a term of imprisonment of up
    to five years; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 122(1)(b) or (c) is guilty of an offence
    and liable on conviction on indictment to a term of imprisonment of
    up to 14 years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggravating factors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The court, in determining the penalty to be imposed,
  shall take into account whether

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the commission of the offence was for the benefit
    of, at the direction of or in association with a criminal
    organization as defined in subsection 121.1(1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the commission of the offence was for profit,
    whether or not any profit was realized.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 123
- 2012, c. 17, s. 44
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-123-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 123"){.PITLink}
:::

### [General Offences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275952 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of Act

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person commits an offence who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contravenes a provision of this Act for which a
    penalty is not specifically provided or fails to comply with a
    condition or obligation imposed under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} escapes or attempts to escape from lawful custody
    or detention under this Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} employs a foreign national in a capacity in which
    the foreign national is not authorized under this Act to be
    employed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed knowledge

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(c), a person who
  fails to exercise due diligence to determine whether employment is
  authorized under this Act is deemed to know that it is not authorized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence defence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person referred to in subsection 148(1) shall not
  be found guilty of an offence under paragraph (1)(a) if it is
  established that they exercised all due diligence to prevent the
  commission of the offence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalties

**[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** A person who commits an
offence under subsection 124(1) is liable

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine of not more
  than \$50,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than two
  years, or to both; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine of not more than
  \$10,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than six months, or
  to both.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Counselling misrepresentation

**[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
knowingly counsels, induces, aids or abets or attempts to counsel,
induce, aid or abet any person to directly or indirectly misrepresent or
withhold material facts relating to a relevant matter that induces or
could induce an error in the administration of this Act is guilty of an
offence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Misrepresentation

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** No person shall
knowingly

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} directly or indirectly misrepresent or withhold
  material facts relating to a relevant matter that induces or could
  induce an error in the administration of this Act;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} communicate, directly or indirectly, by any means,
  false or misleading information or declarations with intent to induce
  or deter immigration to Canada; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} refuse to be sworn or to affirm or declare, as the
  case may be, or to answer a question put to the person at an
  examination or at a proceeding held under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 127
- 2015, c. 3, s. 115(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-127-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 127"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Penalties

**[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** A person who
contravenes a provision of section 126 or 127 is guilty of an offence
and liable

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine of not more
  than \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than five
  years, or to both; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine of not more than
  \$50,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than two years, or
  to both.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences relating to officers

- **[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person is guilty of an offence
  who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} being an officer or an employee of the Government
    of Canada, knowingly makes or issues any false document or
    statement, or accepts or agrees to accept a bribe or other benefit,
    in respect of any matter under this Act or knowingly fails to
    perform their duties under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} gives or offers to give a bribe or consideration
    to, or makes an agreement or arrangement with, an officer to induce
    the officer not to perform their duties under this Act;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} falsely personates an officer or by any act or
    omission leads any person to believe that the person is an officer;
    or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} obstructs or impedes an officer in the performance
    of the officer's duties under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who is guilty of an offence under
  subsection (1) is liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine of not more
    than \$50,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than five
    years, or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine of not more than
    \$10,000 or to imprisonment for a term of not more than six months,
    or to both.

**[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2001, c.
32, s. 81\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Counselling offence

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
knowingly induces, aids or abets or attempts to induce, aid or abet any
person to contravene section 117, 118, 119, 122, 124 or 129, or who
counsels a person to do so, commits an offence and is liable to the same
penalty as that person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 131, c. 32, s. 81
- 2012, c. 17, s. 45(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-131-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 131"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2001, c.
32, s. 81\]]{.Repealed}

### [Prosecution of Offences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-275997 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deferral

**[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** A person who has
claimed refugee protection, and who came to Canada directly or
indirectly from the country in respect of which the claim is made, may
not be charged with an offence under section 122, paragraph 124(1)(a) or
section 127 of this Act or under section 57, paragraph 340(c) or section
354, 366, 368, 374 or 403 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), in
relation to the coming into Canada of the person, pending disposition of
their claim for refugee protection or if refugee protection is
conferred.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period for summary conviction
offences

- **[[133.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A proceeding by way of summary
  conviction for an offence under section 117, 126 or 127, or section
  131 as it relates to section 117, may be instituted at any time
  within, but not later than, 10 years after the day on which the
  subject-matter of the proceeding arose, and a proceeding by way of
  summary conviction for any other offence under this Act may be
  instituted at any time within, but not later than, five years after
  the day on which the subject-matter of the proceeding arose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the subject-matter
  of the proceeding arose before the day on which this section comes
  into force.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2011, c. 8, s. 3
- 2012, c. 17, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-133.1-20110630.html "Link to previous version of section 133.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence --- incorporation by reference

**[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** No person may be found
guilty of an offence or subjected to a penalty for the contravention of
a provision of a regulation that incorporates material by reference,
unless it is proved that, at the time of the alleged contravention,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the material was reasonably accessible to the person;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} reasonable steps had been taken to ensure that the
  material was accessible to persons likely to be affected by the
  regulation; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the material had been published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences outside Canada

**[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** An act or omission that
would by reason of this Act be punishable as an offence if committed in
Canada is, if committed outside Canada, an offence under this Act and
may be tried and punished in Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Venue

- **[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  proceeding in respect of an offence under this Act may be instituted,
  tried and determined at the place in Canada where the offence was
  committed or at the place in Canada where the person charged with the
  offence is or has an office or place of business at the time of the
  institution of those proceedings.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where commission outside Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A proceeding in respect of an offence under this Act
  that is committed outside Canada may be instituted, tried and
  determined at any place in Canada.

### [Forfeiture]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276019 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forfeiture

- **[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  court that convicts a person of an offence under this Act may, in
  addition to any other punishment imposed, order that any
  offence-related property seized in relation to the offence be
  forfeited to Her Majesty in right of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may define the expression
  "offence-related property" for the purposes of this section, may
  provide for any matter relating to the application of this section,
  and may include provisions respecting the return to their lawful
  owner, disposition, or disposition of the proceeds of disposition, of
  offence-related property that has been seized.

### [Officers Authorized to Enforce Act]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276025 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of peace officer

- **[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer, if so authorized, has the authority and powers of a peace
  officer --- including those set out in sections 487 to 492.2 of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) --- to enforce this Act, including any
  of its provisions with respect to the arrest, detention or removal
  from Canada of any person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary assistants

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer may, in cases of emergency, employ a
  person to assist the officer in carrying out duties under this Act.
  That person has the authority and powers of the officer for a period
  of no more than 48 hours, unless approved by the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Search

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer may search any person seeking to come into Canada and may
  search their luggage and personal effects and the means of
  transportation that conveyed the person to Canada if the officer
  believes on reasonable grounds that the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has not revealed their identity or has hidden on or
    about their person documents that are relevant to their
    admissibility; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has committed, or possesses documents that may be
    used in the commission of, an offence referred to in section 117,
    118 or 122.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Search by person of same sex

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A search of a person under this section must be
  performed by a person of the same sex as the person being searched. If
  an officer of the same sex is not available, any suitable person of
  the same sex may be authorized by an officer to perform the search.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure

- **[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  officer may seize and hold any means of transportation, document or
  other thing if the officer believes on reasonable grounds that it was
  fraudulently or improperly obtained or used or that the seizure is
  necessary to prevent its fraudulent or improper use or to carry out
  the purposes of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection 42(2) of the [Canada Post
  Corporation Act](/eng/acts/C-10), a thing or document that is detained
  under the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6) and seized by an officer is
  not in the course of post for the purposes of the [Canada Post
  Corporation Act](/eng/acts/C-10).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may provide for any matter relating
  to the application of this section and may include provisions
  respecting the deposit of security as a guarantee to replace things
  that have been seized or that might otherwise be seized, and the
  return to their lawful owner, and the disposition, of things that have
  been seized.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oaths and evidence

**[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** Every officer has the
authority to administer oaths and to take and receive evidence under
oath on any matter arising out of this Act.

### [Peace Officers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276047 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of peace officers to execute
orders

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every peace officer and every person
  in immediate charge or control of an immigrant station shall, when so
  directed by an officer, execute any warrant or written order issued
  under this Act for the arrest, detention or removal from Canada of any
  permanent resident or foreign national.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[immigrant
  station]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[immigrant
  station]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, subject to subsection (3), a
  facility that is operated by the Canada Border Services Agency --- or
  used by a peace officer or any party to an agreement or arrangement
  entered into under paragraph 13(2)(b) of the [Canada Border Services
  Agency Act](/eng/acts/C-1.4) --- for the purpose of detaining persons
  under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Area of a penitentiary

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An area of a [[penitentiary]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
  as defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Corrections and Conditional
  Release Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), may be an immigrant station only if it
  is designated under subsection 94.4(1) of that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person detained under this Act must not be detained
  in a [[designated immigrant station]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as
  defined in section 94.1 of the [Corrections and Conditional Release
  Act](/eng/acts/C-44.6), unless, subject to subsections (5) to (7), the
  Minister determines that the person is to be detained in a designated
  immigrant station because the person requires a high degree of
  supervision and control, based on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature and level of the danger to the public
    that the person poses, having regard to any of the following
    factors:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any conviction in Canada under an Act of
      Parliament for a sexual offence or an offence involving violence
      or weapons,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any conviction outside Canada for an offence
      that, if committed in Canada, would constitute a sexual offence or
      an offence involving violence or weapons under an Act of
      Parliament,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any pending charges in Canada under an Act of
      Parliament for a sexual offence or an offence involving violence
      or weapons,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any pending charges outside Canada, for an
      offence that, if committed in Canada, would constitute a sexual
      offence or an offence involving violence or weapons under an Act
      of Parliament,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} association with a [[criminal
      organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, within the meaning of
      subsection 121.1(1), or

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} association with an organization that engages,
      or has engaged, in terrorism; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any serious non-compliance by the person with the
    rules, applicable in an immigrant station, any other detention
    facility or any correctional facility where the person is or has
    been detained, with respect to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the possession of weapons or the possession of or
      dealing in [[controlled substances]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as
      defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Controlled Drugs and Substances
      Act](/eng/acts/C-38.8), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} violent or aggressive behaviour towards any
      other person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minor children

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must not determine that a minor child
  detained under this Act is to be detained in a designated immigrant
  station.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements prior to determination

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Before determining that a person detained under this
  Act is to be detained in a designated immigrant station, the Minister
  must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide the person with a written notice informing
    them that the Minister is considering making a determination that
    the person is to be detained in a designated immigrant station and
    informing them of the right to make submissions and to retain and
    instruct counsel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allow the person a reasonable opportunity to
    exercise the rights referred in paragraph (a); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} consider the person's state of health and health
    care needs, including with respect to their mental health.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written reasons

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister determines that a person detained
  under this Act is to be detained in a designated immigrant station,
  the Minister must provide the person with written reasons for the
  determination before the person is detained in a designated immigrant
  station.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergencies

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (6) and (7) do not apply in an emergency
  in which a person detained under the Act must be immediately detained
  in a designated immigrant station because of a risk to the safety of
  the person or to other persons in, or a risk to the security of, the
  immigrant station where the person is or would otherwise be detained.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of person detained

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If a person is detained in a designated immigrant
  station in the circumstances described in subsection (8), the Minister
  must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as soon as feasible after the person is detained in
    the designated immigrant station, provide the person with a written
    notice informing them that the Minister is considering making a
    determination that the person is to remain detained in a designated
    immigrant station and informing them of the right to make
    submissions and to retain and instruct counsel; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allow the person a reasonable opportunity to
    exercise the rights referred in paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written reasons

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister determines that a person detained in
  a designated immigrant station in the circumstances described in
  subsection (8) is to remain detained in the designated immigrant
  station, the Minister must provide the person with written reasons for
  the determination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 142
- 2015, c. 3, s. 116(F)
- [2024, c. 17, s. 390](#1468466-1480027){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1468466-1480027.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-142-20150226.html "Link to previous version of section 142"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to execute warrants and orders

**[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** A warrant issued or an
order to detain made under this Act is, notwithstanding any other law,
sufficient authority to the person to whom it is addressed or who may
receive and execute it to arrest and detain the person with respect to
whom the warrant or order was issued or made.

### [Ticketable Offences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276054 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prosecution of designated offences

- **[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  addition to other procedures set out in this Act or in the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) for commencing a proceeding, proceedings in
  respect of any offence that is prescribed by regulation may be
  commenced in accordance with this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An officer may commence a proceeding by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} completing a ticket that consists of a summons
    portion and an information portion;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} delivering the summons portion of the ticket to the
    accused or mailing it to the accused at the accused's latest known
    address; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} filing the information portion of the ticket with a
    court of competent jurisdiction before or as soon as practicable
    after the summons portion has been delivered or mailed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of ticket

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The summons and information portions of a ticket must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} set out a description of the offence and the time
    and place of its alleged commission;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} include a statement, signed by the officer, that
    there are reasonable grounds to believe that the accused committed
    the offence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the amount of the prescribed fine for the
    offence and the manner in which and period within which it must be
    paid;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} include a statement that, if the accused pays the
    fine within the period set out in the ticket, a conviction will be
    entered and recorded against the accused; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} include a statement that if the accused wishes to
    plead not guilty or for any other reason fails to pay the fine
    within the period set out in the ticket, the accused must appear in
    the court and at the time set out in the ticket.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consequences of payment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Payment of the fine by the accused within the period
  set out in the ticket constitutes a plea of guilty to the offence
  described in the ticket and, following the payment,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a conviction shall be entered against the accused
    and no further action shall be taken against the accused in respect
    of that offence; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any thing seized from the accused under this Act
    relating to the offence described in the ticket, or any proceeds
    realized from its disposition, are forfeited to Her Majesty in right
    of Canada and may be disposed of as the Minister directs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may provide for any matter relating
  to the application of this section, and may include provisions
  prescribing

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offences referred to in subsection (1) and the
    manner in which those offences may be described in tickets; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the fine, not exceeding \$10,000, for
    a prescribed offence.

### [Debt Due to Her Majesty]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276078 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debts due

- **[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following amounts are debts due to Her Majesty in right of Canada
  payable on demand:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a debt incurred by Her Majesty for which any person
    is liable under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an amount that a person has agreed to pay as a
    deposit or guarantee of performance of an obligation under this Act;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} the amount of a penalty imposed under any
    regulation made under paragraph 32(d.4);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the costs incurred in removing a prescribed foreign
    national from Canada;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an amount that is ordered to be paid under section
    147 on account of an unpaid liability; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an amount referred to in paragraph 148(1)(g).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debts due --- sponsors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any federal-provincial agreement, an
  amount that a sponsor is required to pay under the terms of an
  undertaking is payable on demand to Her Majesty in right of Canada and
  Her Majesty in right of the province concerned and may be recovered by
  Her Majesty in either or both of those rights.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of debt

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A debt may be recovered at any time.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 145
- 2014, c. 20, s. 304
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-145-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 145"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Collection of Debts Due to Her Majesty]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276094 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificates

- **[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  amount or part of an amount payable under this Act that has not been
  paid may be certified by the Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} without delay, if the Minister is of the opinion
    that the person liable for that amount is attempting to avoid
    payment; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, on the expiration of 30 days
    after the default.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister of Employment and Social
  Development

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} When a penalty is imposed as a result of the
  Minister of Employment and Social Development exercising a power
  conferred on him or her by regulation made under paragraph 32(d.4),
  that Minister is responsible for the recovery of the debt referred to
  in paragraph 145(1)(b.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judgments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The certificate is to be filed and registered in the
  Federal Court and, when registered, has the same force and effect, and
  all proceedings may be taken, as if the certificate were a judgment
  obtained in the Court for a debt of the amount specified in the
  certificate plus interest to the day of payment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The costs of registering the certificate are
  recoverable in the same manner as if they had been included in the
  certificate.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 146
- 2014, c. 20, s. 305
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-146-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 146"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Garnishment

- **[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister is of the opinion that a person is or is about to become
  liable to make a payment to a person liable to make a payment under
  this Act, the Minister may, by written notice, order the first person
  to pay to the Receiver General, on account of the second person's
  liability, all or part of the money otherwise payable to the second
  person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicability to future payments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister, under subsection (1), orders an
  employer to pay to the Receiver General money otherwise payable to an
  employee as remuneration,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the order is applicable to all future payments of
    remuneration until the liability is satisfied; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employer shall pay to the Receiver General out
    of each payment of remuneration the amount that the Minister
    stipulates in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discharge of liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The receipt of the Minister is a good and sufficient
  discharge of the original liability to the extent of the payment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may provide for any matter relating
  to the application of this section.

### [Transportation Companies]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276119 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation of operators of vehicles and
facilities

- **[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who owns or operates a vehicle or a transportation facility,
  and an agent for such a person, must, in accordance with the
  regulations,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not carry to Canada a person who is prescribed or
    does not hold a prescribed document, or whom an officer directs not
    to be carried;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} hold the prescribed documentation of a person whom
    it carries to Canada until an examination begins, present the person
    for examination and hold the person until the examination is
    completed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} arrange for a medical examination and medical
    treatment and observation of a person it carries to Canada;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} provide prescribed information, including
    documentation and reports;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} provide facilities for the holding and examination
    of persons being carried to Canada;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} carry from Canada a person whom it has carried to
    or caused to enter Canada and who is prescribed or whom an officer
    directs to be carried;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} pay for all prescribed costs and fees relating to
    paragraphs (a), (b), (c) and (f); and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} provide security for compliance with its
    obligations under paragraphs (a) to (g).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure of security for compliance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person who owns or operates a vehicle or a
  transportation facility, or an agent of such a person, fails to comply
  with an obligation under this Act, all or part of any security
  provided by the person and any vehicle or other prescribed good owned
  or operated by the person may be detained, seized or forfeited to Her
  Majesty in right of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 148
- 2017, c. 26, s. 45
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-148-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 148"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of information

**[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** The following
provisions govern information provided under paragraph 148(1)(d):

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the information may be used only for the purposes of
  this Act or the [Department of Citizenship and Immigration
  Act](/eng/acts/C-29.4) or to identify a person for whom a warrant of
  arrest has been issued in Canada; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} notice regarding use of the information must be given
  to the person to whom it relates.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 149
- 2004, c. 15, s. 71
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-149-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 149"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** The regulations may
provide for any matter relating to the purposes of sections 148 and 149,
may define, for the purposes of this Act, terms used in those sections
and may include provisions respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the requirements and procedures applicable to a
  person who owns or operates a vehicle or a transportation facility;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the costs and fees for which a person who owns or
  operates a vehicle or a transportation facility is liable;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to be followed when a vehicle or other
  security is detained, seized, forfeited to Her Majesty in right of
  Canada or returned; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the procedures by which a person may make claim that
  their interest in a vehicle or other good is not affected by it being
  detained, seized or forfeited to Her Majesty in right of Canada.

### [Sharing of Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276147 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[150.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may provide for any
  matter relating to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the collection, retention, use, disclosure and
    disposal of information, including a Social Insurance Number, for
    the purposes of this Act or for the purposes of program legislation
    as defined in section 2 of the [Canada Border Services Agency
    Act](/eng/acts/C-1.4);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure of information for the purposes of
    national security, the defence of Canada or the conduct of
    international affairs, including the implementation of an agreement
    or arrangement entered into under section 5 or 5.1 of the
    [Department of Citizenship and Immigration Act](/eng/acts/C-29.4) or
    section 13 of the [Canada Border Services Agency
    Act](/eng/acts/C-1.4);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure of information relating to the
    professional or ethical conduct of a person referred to in any of
    paragraphs 91(2)(a) to (c) in connection with a proceeding --- other
    than a proceeding before a superior court --- or application under
    this Act to a body that is responsible for governing or
    investigating that conduct or to a person who is responsible for
    investigating that conduct, for the purposes of ensuring that
    persons referred to in those paragraphs offer and provide
    professional and ethical representation and advice to persons in
    connection with such proceedings and applications;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the retention, use, disclosure and disposal by the
    Royal Canadian Mounted Police of biometric information and any
    related personal information that is collected under this Act and
    provided to it for the enforcement of any law of Canada or a
    province; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure of information for the purposes of
    cooperation between the Government of Canada and the government of a
    province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under subsection (1) may include
  conditions under which the collection, retention, use, disposal and
  disclosure may be made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2004, c. 15, s. 72
- 2005, c. 38, s. 119
- 2011, c. 8, s. 4
- 2012, c. 17, s. 47
