- 2014, c. 39, s. 313
- 2015, c. 36, s. 174
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-150.1-20141216.html "Link to previous version of section 150.1"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Immigration and Refugee Board]{.HTitleText1} {#h-276160 .Part}

### [Composition of Board]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276161 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immigration and Refugee Board

**[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** The Immigration and
Refugee Board consists of the Refugee Protection Division, the Refugee
Appeal Division, the Immigration Division and the Immigration Appeal
Division.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition

**[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** The Board is composed
of a Chairperson and other members as are required to ensure the proper
functioning of the Board.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Oath or affirmation of office

**[[152.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152.1 .sectionLabel}** The Chairperson and
other members of the Board must swear the oath or give the solemn
affirmation of office set out in the rules of the Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 8, s. 17
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson and other members

- **[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chairperson and members of the Refugee Appeal Division and Immigration
  Appeal Division

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are appointed to the Board by the Governor in
    Council, to hold office during good behaviour for a term not
    exceeding seven years, subject to removal by the Governor in Council
    at any time for cause, to serve in a regional or district office of
    the Board;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2010, c. 8, s. 18\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} are eligible for reappointment in the same or
    another capacity;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} shall receive the remuneration that may be fixed by
    the Governor in Council;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} are entitled to be paid reasonable travel and
    living expenses incurred while absent in the course of their duties,
    in the case of a full-time member, from their ordinary place of work
    or, in the case of a part-time member, while absent from their
    ordinary place of residence;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} are deemed to be employed in the public service for
    the purposes of the [Public Service Superannuation
    Act](/eng/acts/P-36) and in the federal public administration for
    the purposes of the [Government Employees Compensation
    Act](/eng/acts/G-5) and any regulations made under section 9 of the
    [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} may not accept or hold any office or employment or
    carry on any activity inconsistent with their duties and functions
    under this Act; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} if appointed as full-time members, must devote the
    whole of their time to the performance of their duties under this
    Act.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 17, s. 84\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deputy Chairperson and Assistant Deputy
  Chairpersons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} One Deputy Chairperson for each Division referred to
  in subsection (1) and not more than 10 Assistant Deputy Chairpersons
  are to be designated by the Governor in Council from among the
  full-time members of those Divisions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Full-time and part-time appointments

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson and the Deputy Chairpersons and
  Assistant Deputy Chairpersons of the Divisions referred to in
  subsection (1) are appointed on a full-time basis and the other
  members are appointed on a full-time or part-time basis.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualification

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Deputy Chairperson of the Immigration Appeal
  Division and a majority of the Assistant Deputy Chairpersons of that
  Division and at least 10 per cent of the members of the Divisions
  referred to in subsection (1) must be members of at least five years
  standing at the bar of a province or notaries of at least five years
  standing at the [Chambre des notaires du Québec]{.otherLang
  lang="fr"}.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 153
- 2003, c. 22, s. 173
- 2010, c. 8, s. 18
- 2012, c. 17, ss. 48, 84
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-153-20120628.html "Link to previous version of section 153"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition after member ceases to hold
office

**[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** A former member of the
Board, within eight weeks after ceasing to be a member, may make or take
part in a decision on a matter that they heard as a member, if the
Chairperson so requests. For that purpose, the former member is deemed
to be a member.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition if member unable to take part

**[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155 .sectionLabel}** If a member of a
three-member panel is unable to take part in the disposition of a matter
that the member has heard, the remaining members may make the
disposition and, for that purpose, are deemed to constitute the
applicable Division.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity and no summons

**[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** The following rules
apply to the Chairperson and the members in respect of the exercise or
purported exercise of their functions under this Act:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} no criminal or civil proceedings lie against them for
  anything done or omitted to be done in good faith; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} they are not competent or compellable to appear as a
  witness in any civil proceedings.

### [Head Office and Staff]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276198 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Head office

- **[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  head office of the Board shall be in the National Capital Region as
  described in the schedule to the [National Capital
  Act](/eng/acts/N-4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Residence --- Chairperson

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson must live in the National Capital
  Region or within reasonable commuting distance of it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personnel

**[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** The Executive Director
and other personnel necessary for the proper conduct of the business of
the Board shall be appointed in accordance with the [Public Service
Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01), and the personnel are deemed to be
employed in the public service for the purposes of the [Public Service
Superannuation Act](/eng/acts/P-36).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 158
- 2003, c. 22, s. 225(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-158-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 158"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Duties of Chairperson]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276208 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson

- **[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chairperson is, by virtue of holding that office, a member of each
  Division of the Board and is the chief executive officer of the Board.
  In that capacity, the Chairperson

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has supervision over and direction of the work and
    staff of the Board;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may at any time assign a member appointed under
    paragraph 153(1)(a) to the Refugee Appeal Division or the
    Immigration Appeal Division;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} may at any time, despite paragraph 153(1)(a),
    assign a member of the Refugee Appeal Division or the Immigration
    Appeal Division to work in another regional or district office to
    satisfy operational requirements, but an assignment may not exceed
    120 days without the approval of the Governor in Council;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} may designate, from among the full-time members
    appointed under paragraph 153(1)(a), coordinating members for the
    Refugee Appeal Division or the Immigration Appeal Division;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} assigns administrative functions to the members of
    the Board;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} apportions work among the members of the Board and
    fixes the place, date and time of proceedings;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} takes any action that may be necessary to ensure
    that the members of the Board carry out their duties efficiently and
    without undue delay;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} may issue guidelines in writing to members of the
    Board and identify decisions of the Board as jurisprudential guides,
    after consulting with the Deputy Chairpersons, to assist members in
    carrying out their duties; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} may appoint and, subject to the approval of the
    Treasury Board, fix the remuneration of experts or persons having
    special knowledge to assist the Divisions in any matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson may delegate any of his or her powers
  under this Act to a member of the Board, except that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} powers referred to in subsection 161(1) may not be
    delegated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} powers referred to in paragraphs (1)(a) and (i) may
    be delegated to the Executive Director of the Board;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} powers in relation to the Immigration Appeal
    Division and the Refugee Appeal Division may only be delegated to
    the Deputy Chairperson, the Assistant Deputy Chairpersons, or other
    members, including coordinating members, of either of those
    Divisions; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} powers in relation to the Immigration Division or
    the Refugee Protection Division may only be delegated to the Deputy
    Chairperson, the Assistant Deputy Chairpersons or other members,
    including coordinating members, of that Division.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 159
- 2010, c. 8, s. 19
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-159-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 159"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence, incapacity or vacancy

**[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** In the event of the
absence or incapacity of the Chairperson, or if the office of
Chairperson is vacant, the Minister may authorize one of the Deputy
Chairpersons or any other member of the Board to act as Chairperson.

### [Functioning of Board]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276231 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules

- **[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the approval of the
  Governor in Council, and in consultation with the Deputy Chairpersons,
  the Chairperson may make rules respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the referral of a claim for refugee protection to
    the Refugee Protection Division;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the factors to be taken into account in fixing or
    changing the date of the hearing referred to in subsection 100(4.1);

  - [(a.2)]{.lawlabel} the activities, practice and procedure of each of
    the Divisions of the Board, including the periods for appeal, other
    than in respect of appeals of decisions of the Refugee Protection
    Division, the priority to be given to proceedings, the notice that
    is required and the period in which notice must be given;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of persons in proceedings before the
    Board, as well as the consequences of, and sanctions for, the breach
    of those rules;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the information that may be required and the manner
    in which, and the time within which, it must be provided with
    respect to a proceeding before the Board; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other matter considered by the Chairperson to
    require rules.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Distinctions

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The rules made under paragraph (1)(c) may
  distinguish among claimants for refugee protection who make their
  claims inside Canada on the basis of whether their claims are made at
  a port of entry or elsewhere or on the basis of whether they are
  nationals of a country that is, on the day on which their claim is
  made, a country designated under subsection 109.1(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling in Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall cause a copy of any rule made
  under subsection (1) to be laid before each House of Parliament on any
  of the first 15 days on which that House is sitting after the approval
  of the rule by the Governor in Council.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 161
- 2010, c. 8, s. 20
- 2012, c. 17, ss. 49, 61, 84
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-161-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 161"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Provisions that Apply to All Divisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276247 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sole and exclusive jurisdiction

- **[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Each
  Division of the Board has, in respect of proceedings brought before it
  under this Act, sole and exclusive jurisdiction to hear and determine
  all questions of law and fact, including questions of jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each Division shall deal with all proceedings before
  it as informally and quickly as the circumstances and the
  considerations of fairness and natural justice permit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of panels

**[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** Matters before a
Division shall be conducted before a single member unless, except for
matters before the Immigration Division, the Chairperson is of the
opinion that a panel of three members should be constituted.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presence of parties

**[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** Where a hearing is held
by a Division, it may, in the Division's discretion, be conducted in the
presence of, or by a means of live telecommunication with, the person
who is the subject of the proceedings.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of a commissioner

**[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** The Refugee Protection
Division, the Refugee Appeal Division and the Immigration Division and
each member of those Divisions have the powers and authority of a
commissioner appointed under Part I of the [Inquiries
Act](/eng/acts/I-11) and may do any other thing they consider necessary
to provide a full and proper hearing.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 165
- 2010, c. 8, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-165-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 165"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceedings --- all Divisions

**[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166 .sectionLabel}** Proceedings before a
Division are to be conducted as follows:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to the other provisions of this section,
  proceedings must be held in public;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on application or on its own initiative, the Division
  may conduct a proceeding in the absence of the public, or take any
  other measure that it considers necessary to ensure the
  confidentiality of the proceedings, if, after having considered all
  available alternate measures, the Division is satisfied that there is

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a serious possibility that the life, liberty or
    security of a person will be endangered if the proceeding is held in
    public,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a real and substantial risk to the fairness of the
    proceeding such that the need to prevent disclosure outweighs the
    societal interest that the proceeding be conducted in public, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a real and substantial risk that matters
    involving public security will be disclosed;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph (d), proceedings before the
  Refugee Protection Division and the Refugee Appeal Division must be
  held in the absence of the public;

- [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} subject to paragraph (d), proceedings before the
  Immigration Division must be held in the absence of the public if they
  concern a person who is the subject of a proceeding before the Refugee
  Protection Division or the Refugee Appeal Division that is pending or
  who has made an application for protection to the Minister that is
  pending;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} on application or on its own initiative, the Division
  may conduct a proceeding in public, or take any other measure that it
  considers necessary to ensure the appropriate access to the
  proceedings if, after having considered all available alternate
  measures and the factors set out in paragraph (b), the Division is
  satisfied that it is appropriate to do so;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} despite paragraphs (b) to (c.1), a representative or
  agent of the United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees is entitled
  to observe proceedings concerning a protected person or a person who
  has made a claim for refugee protection or an application for
  protection; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} despite paragraph (e), the representative or agent
  may not observe any part of the proceedings that deals with
  information or other evidence in respect of which an application has
  been made under section 86, and not rejected, or with information or
  other evidence protected under that section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 166
- 2008, c. 3, s. 5
- 2012, c. 17, s. 50
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-166-20080222.html "Link to previous version of section 166"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to counsel

- **[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who is the subject of proceedings before any Division of the
  Board and the Minister may, at their own expense, be represented by
  legal or other counsel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person who is the subject of proceedings is
  under 18 years of age or unable, in the opinion of the applicable
  Division, to appreciate the nature of the proceedings, the Division
  shall designate a person to represent the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 167
- 2010, c. 8, s. 23
- 2012, c. 17, s. 63
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-167-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 167"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abandonment of proceeding

- **[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Division may determine that a proceeding before it has been abandoned
  if the Division is of the opinion that the applicant is in default in
  the proceedings, including by failing to appear for a hearing, to
  provide information required by the Division or to communicate with
  the Division on being requested to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abuse of process

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Division may refuse to allow an applicant to
  withdraw from a proceeding if it is of the opinion that the withdrawal
  would be an abuse of process under its rules.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decisions and reasons

**[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** In the case of a
decision of a Division, other than an interlocutory decision:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the decision takes effect in accordance with the
  rules;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} reasons for the decision must be given;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the decision may be rendered orally or in writing,
  except a decision of the Refugee Appeal Division, which must be
  rendered in writing;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the Refugee Protection Division rejects a claim,
  written reasons must be provided to the claimant and the Minister;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the person who is the subject of proceedings
  before the Board or the Minister requests reasons for a decision
  within 10 days of notification of the decision, or in circumstances
  set out in the rules of the Board, the Division must provide written
  reasons; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} the period in which to apply for judicial review with
  respect to a decision of the Board is calculated from the giving of
  notice of the decision or from the sending of written reasons,
  whichever is later.

### [Refugee Protection Division]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276295 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition

- **[[169.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Refugee Protection Division
  consists of the Deputy Chairperson, Assistant Deputy Chairpersons and
  other members, including coordinating members, necessary to carry out
  its functions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The members of the Refugee Protection Division are
  appointed in accordance with the [Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 8, s. 26
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceedings

**[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** The Refugee Protection
Division, in any proceeding before it,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} may inquire into any matter that it considers
  relevant to establishing whether a claim is well-founded;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} must hold a hearing;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} must notify the person who is the subject of the
  proceeding and the Minister of the hearing;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} must provide the Minister, on request, with the
  documents and information referred to in subsection 100(4);

- [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} may question the witnesses, including the person
  who is the subject of the proceeding;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} must give the person and the Minister a reasonable
  opportunity to present evidence, question witnesses and make
  representations;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} may, despite paragraph (b), allow a claim for refugee
  protection without a hearing, if the Minister has not notified the
  Division, within the period set out in the rules of the Board, of the
  Minister's intention to intervene;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} is not bound by any legal or technical rules of
  evidence;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} may receive and base a decision on evidence that is
  adduced in the proceedings and considered credible or trustworthy in
  the circumstances; and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} may take notice of any facts that may be judicially
  noticed, any other generally recognized facts and any information or
  opinion that is within its specialized knowledge.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 170
- 2010, c. 8, s. 27
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-170-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 170"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No reopening of claim or application

**[[170.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170.2 .sectionLabel}** The Refugee
Protection Division does not have jurisdiction to reopen on any ground
--- including a failure to observe a principle of natural justice --- a
claim for refugee protection, an application for protection or an
application for cessation or vacation, in respect of which the Refugee
Appeal Division or the Federal Court, as the case may be, has made a
final determination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 17, s. 51
:::

### [Refugee Appeal Division]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276321 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceedings

**[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** In the case of a
proceeding of the Refugee Appeal Division,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Division must give notice of any hearing to the
  Minister and to the person who is the subject of the appeal;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection 110(4), if a hearing is held,
  the Division must give the person who is the subject of the appeal and
  the Minister the opportunity to present evidence, question witnesses
  and make submissions;

- [(a.2)]{.lawlabel} the Division is not bound by any legal or technical
  rules of evidence;

- [(a.3)]{.lawlabel} the Division may receive and base a decision on
  evidence that is adduced in the proceedings and considered credible or
  trustworthy in the circumstances;

- [(a.4)]{.lawlabel} the Minister may, at any time before the Division
  makes a decision, after giving notice to the Division and to the
  person who is the subject of the appeal, intervene in the appeal;

- [(a.5)]{.lawlabel} the Minister may, at any time before the Division
  makes a decision, submit documentary evidence and make written
  submissions in support of the Minister's appeal or intervention in the
  appeal;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Division may take notice of any facts that may be
  judicially noticed and of any other generally recognized facts and any
  information or opinion that is within its specialized knowledge; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a decision of a panel of three members of the Refugee
  Appeal Division has, for the Refugee Protection Division and for a
  panel of one member of the Refugee Appeal Division, the same
  precedential value as a decision of an appeal court has for a trial
  court.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 171
- 2010, c. 8, s. 28
- 2012, c. 17, s. 52
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-171-20120628.html "Link to previous version of section 171"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No reopening of appeal

**[[171.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171.1 .sectionLabel}** The Refugee Appeal
Division does not have jurisdiction to reopen on any ground ---
including a failure to observe a principle of natural justice --- an
appeal in respect of which the Federal Court has made a final
determination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 17, s. 53
:::

### [Immigration Division]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276338 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition

- **[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Immigration Division consists of the Deputy Chairperson, Assistant
  Deputy Chairpersons and other members necessary to carry out its
  functions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The members of the Immigration Division are appointed
  in accordance with the [Public Service Employment
  Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 172
- 2010, c. 8, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-172-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 172"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceedings

**[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** The Immigration
Division, in any proceeding before it,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} must, where practicable, hold a hearing;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} must give notice of the proceeding to the Minister
  and to the person who is the subject of the proceeding and hear the
  matter without delay;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} is not bound by any legal or technical rules of
  evidence; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} may receive and base a decision on evidence adduced
  in the proceedings that it considers credible or trustworthy in the
  circumstances.

### [Immigration Appeal Division]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276352 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court of record

- **[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Immigration Appeal Division is a court of record and shall have an
  official seal, which shall be judicially noticed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Immigration Appeal Division has all the powers,
  rights and privileges vested in a superior court of record with
  respect to any matter necessary for the exercise of its jurisdiction,
  including the swearing and examination of witnesses, the production
  and inspection of documents and the enforcement of its orders.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceedings

- **[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Immigration Appeal Division, in any proceeding before it,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} must, in the case of an appeal under subsection
    63(4), hold a hearing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is not bound by any legal or technical rules of
    evidence; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} may receive and base a decision on evidence adduced
    in the proceedings that it considers credible or trustworthy in the
    circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presence of permanent resident

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of an appeal by a permanent resident
  under subsection 63(4), the Immigration Appeal Division may, after
  considering submissions from the Minister and the permanent resident
  and if satisfied that the presence of the permanent resident at the
  hearing is necessary, order the permanent resident to physically
  appear at the hearing, in which case an officer shall issue a travel
  document for that purpose.

### [Remedial and Disciplinary Measures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276366 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request

- **[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chairperson may request the Minister to decide whether any member of
  the Immigration Appeal Division or the Refugee Appeal Division should
  be subject to remedial or disciplinary measures for a reason set out
  in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The request is to be based on the reason that the
  member has become incapacitated from the proper execution of that
  office by reason of infirmity, has been guilty of misconduct, has
  failed in the proper execution of that office or has been placed, by
  conduct or otherwise, in a position that is incompatible with due
  execution of that office.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 176
- 2010, c. 8, s. 30
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-176-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 176"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures

**[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** On receipt of the
request, the Minister may take one or more of the following measures:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtain, in an informal and expeditious manner, any
  information that the Minister considers necessary;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} refer the matter for mediation, if the Minister is
  satisfied that the issues in relation to the request may be
  appropriately resolved by mediation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} request of the Governor in Council that an inquiry be
  held under section 178; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} advise the Chairperson that the Minister considers
  that it is not necessary to take further measures under this section
  or sections 178 to 185.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of inquirer

**[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** On receipt of a request
referred to in paragraph 177(c), the Governor in Council may, on the
recommendation of the Minister of Justice, appoint a judge of a superior
court to conduct an inquiry.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

**[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** The judge has all the
powers, rights and privileges that are vested in a superior court,
including the power

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to issue a summons requiring any person to appear at
  the time and place mentioned in the summons to testify about all
  matters within that person's knowledge relative to the inquiry and to
  produce any document or thing relative to the inquiry that the person
  has or controls; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to administer oaths and examine any person on oath.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff

**[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** The judge may engage
the services of counsel and other persons having technical or
specialized knowledge to assist the judge in conducting the inquiry,
establish the terms and conditions of their engagement and, with the
approval of the Treasury Board, fix and pay their remuneration and
expenses.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions to public hearing

- **[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  inquiry must be held in public. However, the judge may, on
  application, take any appropriate measures and make any order that the
  judge considers necessary to ensure the confidentiality of the inquiry
  if, after having considered all available alternate measures, the
  judge is satisfied that there is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a real and substantial risk that matters involving
    public security will be disclosed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a real and substantial risk to the fairness of the
    inquiry such that the need to prevent disclosure outweighs the
    societal interest that the inquiry be conducted in public; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a serious possibility that the life, liberty or
    security of a person will be endangered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the judge considers it appropriate, the judge may
  take any measures and make any order that the judge considers
  necessary to ensure the confidentiality of a hearing held in respect
  of an application under subsection (1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rules of evidence

- **[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  conducting an inquiry, the judge is not bound by any legal or
  technical rules of evidence and may receive, and base a decision on,
  evidence presented in the proceedings that the judge considers
  credible or trustworthy in the circumstances of the case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervenors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An interested party may, with leave of the judge,
  intervene in an inquiry on any terms and conditions that the judge
  considers appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to be heard

**[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** The member who is the
subject of the inquiry shall be given reasonable notice of the
subject-matter of the inquiry and of the time and place of any hearing
and shall be given an opportunity, in person or by counsel, to be heard
at the hearing, to cross-examine witnesses and to present evidence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Minister

- **[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After an inquiry has been completed,
  the judge must submit a report containing the judge's findings and
  recommendations, if any, to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The judge may, for any of the reasons set out in
  subsection 176(2), recommend in the report that the member be
  suspended without pay or removed from office or that any other
  disciplinary measure or any remedial measure be taken.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transmission of report to Governor in
Council

**[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** If the Minister
receives a report of an inquiry in which the judge makes a
recommendation, the Minister shall send the report to the Governor in
Council who may, if the Governor in Council considers it appropriate,
suspend the member without pay, remove the member from office or impose
any other disciplinary measure or any remedial measure.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights not affected

**[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** Nothing in sections 176
to 185 affects any right or power of the Governor in Council in relation
to the removal of a member from office for cause.

## [PART 4.1]{.HLabel1}[Electronic Administration]{.HTitleText1} {#h-276412 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

- **[[186.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may administer this Act
  using electronic means, including as it relates to its enforcement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Part does not apply to the Minister of
  Employment and Social Development in respect of any activity the
  administration of which is the responsibility of that Minister under
  this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, any person or class of persons
  who are designated as officers by the Minister to carry out any
  purpose of this Act may, in the exercise of their powers or the
  performance of their duties and functions, use the electronic means
  that are made available or specified by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a person who has been
  authorized by the Minister to do anything that may be done by the
  Minister under this Act, may do so using the electronic means that are
  made available or specified by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision, determination or examination
  by automated system

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, an electronic system,
  including an automated system, may be used by the Minister to make a
  decision or determination under this Act, or by an officer to make a
  decision or determination or to proceed with an examination under this
  Act, if the system is made available to the officer by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 36, s. 175
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for electronic version

**[[186.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186.2 .sectionLabel}** A requirement under
this Act to provide a signature, or to make an application, request,
claim, decision or determination, or to submit or issue any document, or
to give notice or provide information, or to submit a document in its
original form, is satisfied by its electronic version, if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the electronic version is provided by the electronic
  means, including an electronic system, that are made available or
  specified by the Minister; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other requirements that may be prescribed have
  been met.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 36, s. 175
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[186.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may provide for any
  matter respecting the application of section 186.1 and paragraph
  186.2(b), and may include provisions respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the technology or format to be used, or the
    standards, specifications or processes to be followed, including for
    the making or verifying of an electronic signature and the manner in
    which it is to be used; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date and time when, and the place where, an
    electronic version of an application, request, claim, notice,
    decision, determination, document or any information is deemed to be
    sent or received.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to use electronic means

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may require a foreign national or
  another individual who, or entity that, makes an application, request
  or claim, submits any document or provides information under this Act
  to do so using electronic means, including an electronic system. The
  regulations may also include provisions respecting those means,
  including that system, respecting the circumstances in which that
  application, request or claim may be made, the document may be
  submitted or the information may be provided by other means and
  respecting those other means.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's power

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may prescribe the circumstances in
  which the Minister may require a foreign national or another
  individual who, or an entity that, makes an application, request or
  claim, submits any document or provides information under this Act to
  do so using any means that are specified by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic payments

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} require that payments that are required to be made
    or evidence of payment that is required to be provided under this
    Act must be made or provided by means of an electronic system;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} include provisions respecting such a system,
    respecting the circumstances in which those payments may be made or
    evidence of payments may be provided by other means, and respecting
    those other means; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} include provisions respecting the date and time
    when, and the place where, an electronic payment or evidence of
    payment is deemed to be sent or received.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation by reference

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The regulations may incorporate by reference the
  standards or specifications of any government, person or organization,
  either as they exist on a specified date or as amended from time to
  time.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 36, s. 175
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

**[[186.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186.4 .sectionLabel}** If any provision of
this Act or the regulations authorizes an officer or another individual
to require a foreign national or another individual or an entity to
submit a visa or other document or to provide information, the officer
or individual is not precluded by this Part from requiring the foreign
national, other individual or entity to submit the visa or other
document or to provide the information, as the case may be, in
accordance with that provision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 36, s. 175
:::

## [PART 5]{.HLabel1}[Transitional Provisions, Consequential and Related Amendments, Coordinating Amendments, Repeals and Coming into Force]{.HTitleText1} {#h-276454 .Part}

### [Transitional Provisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-276455 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[former
Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
sections 188 to 201, [[former Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
Immigration Act, chapter I-2 of the Revised Statutes of Canada, 1985,
and, where applicable, the regulations and rules made under it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation

- **[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Immigration and Refugee Board continued by section 57 of the former
  Act is hereby continued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson, Deputy Chairpersons,
  Assistant Deputy Chairpersons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson, Deputy Chairpersons and Assistant
  Deputy Chairpersons appointed under the former Act continue in the
  same capacity with the Board until the expiry or revocation of their
  respective appointments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation --- members

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A member appointed under the former Act to the
  Convention Refugee Determination Division or the Immigration Appeal
  Division continues in office as a member of the Board until the expiry
  or revocation of their appointment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Executive Director

  [[Footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_1){#I-2.5_en_1-ID0EBCAA}[(4)]{.lawlabel} The
  person who, on the coming into force of this section, held the office
  of Executive Director of the Board is deemed to have been appointed to
  that office under section 158, without prejudice to any salary and
  benefits he or she may receive by virtue of having held that office
  before that coming into force.

  - ::: Footnote
    [[Return to footnote
    ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_1-ID0EBCAA){#I-2.5_en_1}\[Note:
    Section 188 in force June 28, 2002, *see* SI/2002-97.\]
    :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

**[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_2){#I-2.5_en_2-ID0EBCA}[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189
.sectionLabel}** Sections 94.6, 102.001 to 102.003 and 107.1 of the
former Act are, despite paragraph 274(a), deemed not to be repealed and
the Minister may exercise any of the powers described in those sections
with respect to any business or fund that was approved by the Minister
before the coming into force of paragraph 274(a).

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_2-ID0EBCA){#I-2.5_en_2}\[Note:
  Paragraph 274(a) in force June 28, 2002, *see* SI/2002-97.\]
  :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of this Act

**[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_3){#I-2.5_en_3-ID0EBCA}[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190
.sectionLabel}** Every application, proceeding or matter under the
former Act that is pending or in progress immediately before the coming
into force of this section shall be governed by this Act on that coming
into force.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_3-ID0EBCA){#I-2.5_en_3}\[Note: Section
  190 in force June 28, 2002, *see* SI/2002-97.\]
  :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convention Refugee Determination Division

**[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_4){#I-2.5_en_4-ID0EBCA}[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191
.sectionLabel}** Every application, proceeding or matter before the
Convention Refugee Determination Division under the former Act that is
pending or in progress immediately before the coming into force of this
section, in respect of which substantive evidence has been adduced but
no decision has been made, shall be continued under the former Act by
the Refugee Protection Division of the Board.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_4-ID0EBCA){#I-2.5_en_4}\[Note: Section
  191 in force June 28, 2002, *see* SI/2002-97.\]
  :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immigration Appeal Division

**[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_5){#I-2.5_en_5-ID0EBCA}[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192
.sectionLabel}** If a notice of appeal has been filed with the
Immigration Appeal Division immediately before the coming into force of
this section, the appeal shall be continued under the former Act by the
Immigration Appeal Division of the Board.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_5-ID0EBCA){#I-2.5_en_5}\[Note: Section
  192 in force June 28, 2002, *see* SI/2002-97.\]
  :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Continuation by Immigration Division

**[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_6){#I-2.5_en_6-ID0EBCA}[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193
.sectionLabel}** Every application, proceeding or matter before the
Adjudication Division under the former Act that is pending or in
progress immediately before the coming into force of this section, in
respect of which substantive evidence has been adduced but no decision
has been made, shall be continued under this Act by the Immigration
Division of the Board.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_6-ID0EBCA){#I-2.5_en_6}\[Note: Section
  193 in force June 28, 2002, *see* SI/2002-97.\]
  :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refugee Protection Division

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** In cases referred to in
section 191, a decision by the Refugee Protection Division following a
hearing that has been commenced by the Convention Refugee Determination
Division is not subject to an appeal under section 110.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Convention Refugee Determination Division

**[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** A decision made by the
Convention Refugee Determination Division before the coming into force
of this section is not subject to an appeal under section 110.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeals

**[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** Despite section 192, an
appeal made to the Immigration Appeal Division before the coming into
force of this section shall be discontinued if the appellant has not
been granted a stay under the former Act and the appeal could not have
been made because of section 64 of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 196
- 2015, c. 3, s. 117(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-196-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 196"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stays

**[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** Despite section 192, if
an appellant who has been granted a stay under the former Act breaches a
condition of the stay, the appellant shall be subject to the provisions
of section 64 and subsection 68(4) of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 197
- 2015, c. 3, s. 117(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-197-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 197"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refugee Protection Division

**[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198 .sectionLabel}** The Refugee Protection
Division has jurisdiction to consider decisions of the Convention
Refugee Determination Division that are set aside by the Federal Court,
the Federal Court of Appeal or the Supreme Court of Canada, and shall
dispose of those matters in accordance with the provisions of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 27, s. 198
- 2002, c. 8, s. 194
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-198-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 198"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Redetermination

**[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** Sections 112 to 114
apply to a redetermination of a decision set aside by the Federal Court
with respect to an application for landing as a member of the
post-determination refugee claimants in Canada class within the meaning
of the Immigration Regulations, 1978.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusion

**[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_9){#I-2.5_en_9-ID0EBCA}[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200
.sectionLabel}** Subsection 31(1) does not apply with respect to persons
who were permanent residents, within the meaning of the former Act, on
the coming into force of this section.

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#I-2.5_en_9-ID0EBCA){#I-2.5_en_9}\[Note: Section
  200 in force June 28, 2002, *see* SI/2002-97.\]
  :::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** The regulations may
provide for measures regarding the transition between the former Act and
this Act, including measures regarding classes of persons who will be
subject in whole or in part to this Act or the former Act and measures
regarding financial and enforcement matters.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terminology

**[[273]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273 .sectionLabel}** Unless the context
requires otherwise, "Immigration Act" is replaced by "[Immigration and
Refugee Protection Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5)" in

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any regulation, as defined in section 2 of the
  [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other instrument made

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the execution of a power conferred by or under
    an Act of Parliament, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} by or under the authority of the Governor in
    Council.

## [SCHEDULE]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsection 2(1))]{.OriginatingRef} {#h-276595 .scheduleLabel}
:::

## Sections E and F of Article 1 of the United Nations Convention Relating to the Status of Refugees {#sections-e-and-f-of-article-1-of-the-united-nations-convention-relating-to-the-status-of-refugees .SchedHeadL1}

- [E]{.lawlabel} This Convention shall not apply to a person who is
  recognized by the competent authorities of the country in which he has
  taken residence as having the rights and obligations which are
  attached to the possession of the nationality of that country.

- [F]{.lawlabel} The provisions of this Convention shall not apply to
  any person with respect to whom there are serious reasons for
  considering that:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} he has committed a crime against peace, a war
    crime, or a crime against humanity, as defined in the international
    instruments drawn up to make provision in respect of such crimes;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} he has committed a serious non-political crime
    outside the country of refuge prior to his admission to that country
    as a refugee;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} he has been guilty of acts contrary to the purposes
    and principles of the United Nations.

## Article 1 of the Convention Against Torture and Other Cruel, Inhuman and Degrading Treatment or Punishment {#article-1-of-the-convention-against-torture-and-other-cruel-inhuman-and-degrading-treatment-or-punishment .SchedHeadL1}

- [1]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Convention, torture means any
  act by which severe pain or suffering, whether physical or mental, is
  intentionally inflicted on a person for such purposes as obtaining
  from him or a third person information or a confession, punishing him
  for an act he or a third person has committed or is suspected of
  having committed, or intimidating or coercing him or a third person,
  or for any reason based on discrimination of any kind, when such pain
  or suffering is inflicted by or at the instigation of or with the
  consent or acquiescence of a public official or other person acting in
  an official capacity. It does not include pain or suffering arising
  only from, inherent in or incidental to lawful sanctions.

- [2]{.lawlabel} This article is without prejudice to any international
  instrument or national legislation which does or may contain
  provisions of wider application.
